# The entities below the floor

Below the programme map sit the institutions, programmes, documents, events, locations and statutes that the board holds but has not placed on the map itself. This file takes the 120 most connected of them, most connected first, with what the board records about each and up to eight of their claims.

## Wright-Patterson Air Force Base, Ohio

Wright-Patterson Air Force Base, Ohio is a location on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 1917; axes C3 A3 D1; 19 connections.

In one line: USAF installation, Greene and Montgomery Counties, Ohio; AFMC and NASIC host site.

### What it is

Wright-Patterson Air Force Base sits immediately east and northeast of Dayton, Ohio, straddling Greene and Montgomery Counties, and was formally constituted on 13 January 1948 by consolidating Wright Field, Patterson Field, Dayton Army Air Field and Clinton Army Air Field into a single installation. Wright Field had been a flying field since 22 May 1917; Patterson Field was named on 1 July 1931 for Lt. Frank Stuart Patterson. The base's significance to this board is not its size but its function: from 1945 onward it was the address of American foreign materiel exploitation. The Air Materiel Command T-2 intelligence organisation here received the Operation Paperclip haul; T-2 became the Air Technical Intelligence Center in 1951, ATIC became the Foreign Technology Division in 1961, FTD became the Foreign Aerospace Science and Technology Center in 1991 and then the National Air and Space Intelligence Center in 1993, which is still headquartered here. Every official Air Force UFO project - SIGN (1948), GRUDGE (1949) and Blue Book (1952-1969) - was hosted at this base, under the command that dismantles recovered foreign hardware, not under a science agency or an air defence command. Lt. Gen. Nathan Twining wrote the 23 September 1947 'AMC Opinion Concerning Flying Discs' as commander of Air Materiel Command here; Col. Howard McCoy ran T-2 intelligence here; Capt. Edward Ruppelt ran Blue Book here; J. Allen Hynek consulted here. The 1969 Bolender memo, written by Brig. Gen. Carroll Bolender, closed Blue Book while stating that reports 'which could affect national security' were never part of the Blue Book system - a sentence written about this base's own reporting architecture. 'Hangar 18' in Area B is the popular name attached since at least the 1970s to the alleged storage of Roswell material here; no primary record establishes such storage, and the persistent structural fact is more damning than the folklore: the base's 1947-1951 T-2 intelligence files have never undergone a comprehensive declassification review, and NASIC remains the institutional heir with continuous custody of the filing system. In wave 1 the pseudonymous NASIC-affiliated source 'Jonathan Grey' is placed here.

## Funding Channels

Funding Channels is a domain on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 1947-01-01; axes C1 A3 D2; 13 connections.

In one line: Financial domain - how money reaches an unacknowledged programme and why it cannot be traced.

### What it is

Money is the most auditable thing a concealed programme touches, and this is where the board's hardest evidence should eventually come from. The documented channels are all ordinary: classified programme elements carried in the defence budget as a single line with no descriptive text; general transfer authority, exercised annually under provisions such as Sections 8005 and 8006 of the Department of Defense Appropriations Acts, which lets the Department move substantial sums between accounts after enactment; Military Interdepartmental Purchase Requests, which move money between agencies without a new appropriation; contractor Independent Research and Development, which is company money reimbursed as an allowable overhead cost and is therefore government-funded work with no government line item; and the unvouchered expenditure authority of 50 U.S.C. 3510, under which the head of an agency may account for spending by certificate alone. The reason this is more than theory is that the Department of Defense has never passed a clean financial audit; the DoD Inspector General reported in 2016 that Army General Fund adjustments were not adequately documented or supported, and the audit findings have continued through the FY2025 cycle. Scoring high on this domain means an entity moves, certifies or conceals money.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 55 of 1082, score 75). 13 connections, 11 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A3/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** 50 U.S.C. 3510 permits certain expenditures to be accounted for solely on the certificate of the agency head. Source: 50 U.S.C. 3510
- **Tier 1 documented.** DoD OIG report DODIG-2016-113 found that Army General Fund adjustments were not adequately documented or supported. Source: DODIG-2016-113
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Department of Defense Appropriations Act for 2026 (P.L. 119-75) contains general transfer authority at Sections 8005-8009 permitting post-enactment movement of funds between accounts. Source: P.L. 119-75, DoD Appropriations Act 2026
- **Tier 1 documented.** The DoD Inspector General published its explanation of the FY2025 Department of War financial statement audit as report DOWIG-2026-081 in May 2026. Source: DOWIG-2026-081

## Groom Lake, Nevada

Groom Lake, Nevada is a location on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 1955; axes C2 A3 D2; 13 connections.

In one line: Dry lakebed and USAF/CIA flight test facility, Lincoln County, Nevada; R-4808N; 83 mi NNW of Las Vegas.

### What it is

Groom Lake is a salt flat roughly 3.75 miles north-south by 3 miles east-west at about 4,409 feet elevation in the Groom Lake Valley of Lincoln County, Nevada, 83 miles north-northwest of Las Vegas and 25 miles south of Rachel. The flight test facility on its southwest shore sits inside Restricted Area 4808 North, the 'Groom box' - a 23-by-25-mile block of restricted airspace enclosing the original 6-by-10-mile rectangular withdrawal - and abuts Yucca Flat, where 739 of the 928 Nevada Test Site nuclear detonations occurred. Its acknowledgement history is the reason it belongs on this board as a case study in how the United States conceals a physical installation for decades and then concedes it in fragments. The CIA established the site in April 1955 for Project AQUATONE, the U-2; the first U-2 arrived on 24 July 1955. Project OXCART, the A-12, was established in August 1959, construction of the 8,500-foot runway began in September 1960, the first A-12 arrived 28 February 1962 and flew on 26 April 1962. HAVE BLUE, the Lockheed stealth demonstrator, first flew here in December 1977; the YF-117A first flew on 18 June 1981 with Lockheed test pilot Hal Farley; Northrop's TACIT BLUE first flew 5 February 1982 with Richard G. Thomas. Exploited foreign aircraft were flown here from the late 1960s under HAVE DOUGHNUT, HAVE DRILL, HAVE IDEA and HAVE GLIB. Against that record, the government's disclosure posture is instructive: no acknowledgement of any kind until 1995, when President Clinton issued a presidential determination exempting 'the Air Force's operating location near Groom Lake, Nevada' from environmental disclosure laws - a determination reissued annually since - which arose out of Kasza v. Browner and Frost v. Perry, the 1994 suits by contractors and by the widows of Walter Kasza and Robert Frost alleging exposure to chemicals burned in open pits, in which Rutgers biochemists found elevated dioxin, dibenzofuran and trichloroethylene in claimants' body fat. District Judge Philip Pro rejected the state secrets claim; the suits were dismissed for lack of evidence; the Ninth Circuit affirmed; the Supreme Court declined review. The Air Force acknowledged the site's existence in 1998. Only on 25 June 2013, answering a 2005 FOIA request by Jeffrey T. Richelson of the National Security Archive, did the CIA release the U-2 and OXCART history naming Area 51 and admitting specific activity there. That is fifty-eight years from establishment to specific acknowledgement, achieved by FOIA litigation rather than by voluntary disclosure - the template against which every current UAP disclosure claim should be measured. The cover story function matters too: Groom's own historians note the U-2's high-altitude operations generated a wave of civilian UFO reports in the late 1950s that the Air Force did not correct, meaning a real black programme was knowingly allowed to be absorbed into the UFO explanation space.

### The board's reading

Acknowledgement chronology, stated plainly because it is the board's benchmark: facility established April 1955; no acknowledgement of any kind 1955-1995; 1995 presidential determination exempting 'the Air Force's operating location near Groom Lake, Nevada' from environmental disclosure law, issued in the shadow of Kasza v. Browner and reissued annually; 1998 USAF acknowledgement that the site exists; 25 June 2013 CIA release of the U-2/OXCART history naming Area 51 and admitting specific activity, in response to a FOIA request filed in 2005 by Jeffrey T. Richelson of the National Security Archive. Fifty-eight years from establishment to specific acknowledgement, and the concession was compelled by a records request rather than volunteered. Any claim that a programme of this kind 'could not stay secret' has to be measured against this chronology.

### Why it holds its tier

Source: Presidential determination on the Groom Lake exemption; Wikipedia, Area 51, DEMOTED from Tier 1 (cited to a secondary/encyclopedic source); needs primary replacement

## Materials and custody analysis

Materials and custody analysis is a domain on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 1947; axes C3 A2 D2; 12 connections.

In one line: Cross-cutting domain: physical samples, their analysis and their chain of custody.

### What it is

Materials analysis is the single place where the central claim could be settled by measurement rather than by testimony, which is why both sides of the argument have converged on it. The falsifiable signature is isotopic: terrestrial elements occur in well-characterised isotope ratios set by solar system formation and by known industrial enrichment processes, so a sample whose ratios lie outside that envelope, and outside the known list of processes that can shift them, would be very difficult to explain conventionally. Garry Nolan, Jacques Vallée and colleagues published the methodology for exactly this test in Progress in Aerospace Sciences in 2022, setting out the instrumental techniques - including secondary ion mass spectrometry - applicable to characterising unusual materials for aerospace forensics. The results so far do not support the claim: AARO's January 2026 supplement reports Oak Ridge National Laboratory found a specimen from Ohio to be a conventional aluminium-silicon casting alloy with large grain sizes, interconnected porosity and needle-like silicon precipitates - defects, not exotica. The dual-use route matters here too, because the national laboratories that would perform such analysis are the same ones that perform nuclear forensics under Restricted Data controls. Scoring high on this domain means an entity has held, analysed or controlled physical material.

### The board's reading

Custody chains catalogued in this domain, with the point at which each goes dark. (1) Council Bluffs 1977 - CHAIN COMPLETE: incident with five independent witness groups, police photography, contemporaneous press (Daily Nonpareil 18 Dec 1977; Des Moines Register 26 Jan 1978), initial 1978 identification as high-carbon steel and slag, retention in Jacques Vallee's collection, SIMS and MIBI analysis at Stanford 2021, publication in Progress in Aerospace Sciences 2022. (2) The 'Art's Parts' bismuth/magnesium-zinc laminate - anonymous source to Art Bell (1990s), to Linda Moulton Howe, tested at Carnegie Science's Department of Terrestrial Magnetism (1996), tested by Harold Puthoff on several occasions through a 2012 negative-result letter, purchased by To The Stars Academy for $35,000 (July 2019, SEC filing), placed under a five-year US Army CCDC CRADA (17 October 2019). GOES DARK at the CRADA: no characterisation, no technical output, no Army statement in seven years. (3) The material Eric Davis described in his October 2019 Senate briefings and March 2020 DoD briefing as recovered from 'off-world vehicles not made on this earth'. NO PUBLIC CHAIN AT ALL: no source, no custodian, no location. (4) The human casualty material and imaging that Garry Nolan says government and aerospace-corporation representatives brought to his Stanford laboratory in or before 2021. GOES DARK AT THE FIRST LINK: neither the government element nor the corporation has been named. ## Chain of custody

Chain of custody is a domain on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 1947; axes C3 A2 D2; 11 connections.

In one line: Cross-cutting domain: who held what, when, and under what documentation.

### What it is

The custody domain tracks the documentary handoffs themselves rather than the objects. Its findings on this board are mostly negative and that is the point: To The Stars Academy's own chief operating officer, a former Lockheed Skunk Works advanced systems director, publicly conceded that the ADAM materials had been 'collected from sources with varying levels of chain-of-custody documentation'. The DIA-funded AAWSAP DIRD series is exemplary at documenting the flow of paper - control numbers, fiscal years, named programme managers, contractor addresses - and silent on the flow of objects. No released document in the AAWSAP corpus records the receipt, transfer or storage of a physical sample. That asymmetry is the single most important structural observation in this domain: the government's own released record proves that it commissioned propulsion theory, materials science and injury pathology, and proves nothing about what it was holding while it did so.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 83 of 1082, score 67). 11 connections, 7 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 2 official denials recorded against it. Axes C3/A2/D2.

- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Steve Justice conceded the ADAM materials were 'collected from sources with varying levels of chain-of-custody documentation'. Source: Steve Justice, quoted in Vice, 26 July 2019

## Federal Contracting Mechanics

Federal Contracting Mechanics is a domain on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1947-01-01; axes C2 A2 D1; 10 connections.

In one line: Procedural domain - the vehicles, CRADAs and OTAs through which work is bought and hidden.

### What it is

Follow the contract vehicle and you find the programme, which is why acquisition mechanics belong on an intelligence board. The routes that matter here are the ones with reduced visibility: sole-source justifications under exceptions to full and open competition; Cooperative Research and Development Agreements, which allow a government laboratory to work with a private party on terms that protect the private party's proprietary data; Other Transaction Authority, which sits outside the Federal Acquisition Regulation entirely; and classified contract line items that appear in financial systems only as an obligation figure. The documented case study is the Army's 2019 CRADA with To The Stars Academy to evaluate materials for combat vehicle applications - an agreement reported by Bloomberg Government and examined by The War Zone, in which the mechanism was used for exactly this subject matter in the open. The AAWSAP file shows the ordinary route: a solicitation, a competitive award, priced modifications, and deliverables. Scoring high on this domain means an entity signs, awards or administers these instruments.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 312 of 1082, score 37). 10 connections, 1 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A2/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** DIA awarded the AAWSAP contract following solicitation HHM402-08-R-0211 and issued priced annual modifications, all released under FOIA. Source: DIA AAWSAP contract file, FOIA-00349-2018
- **Tier 1 documented.** The US Army entered a Cooperative Research and Development Agreement with To The Stars Academy in 2019 covering materials evaluation for future combat vehicles. Source: Bloomberg Government, 21 October 2019; The War Zone, 18 October 2019
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Secrecy around the Army-TTSA agreement deepened following initial disclosure, with subsequent records requests returning reduced material. Source: The Black Vault, 'Secrecy Deepens About U.S. Army Agreement To Analyze UFO Material'

### Its strongest connections

- Federal Contracting Mechanics comes in from **Lockheed Martin Corporation** (institution, board tier A), a institutional link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: the prime where classified contract line items and sworn allegation meet. Lockheed Martin's prime contract obligations run to the hundreds of billions of dollars in USAspending records, a portion carried as classified line items visible in financial systems only as a number. It is also the only company named on the floor of a congressional hearing by a sworn witness in connection with the custody allegation, which makes it the test case for whether contracting records can be made to answer the question. ## Independent media as the disclosure substrate

Independent media as the disclosure substrate is a domain on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2017; axes C0 A0 D3; 10 connections.

In one line: Research domain.

### What it is

The domain covering how the disclosure record is actually produced and distributed. The defining fact of the 2023-2026 period is that the substrate moved: the New York Times carried the 2017 AATIP story but not the 2023 Grusch story, which went to The Debrief; the most detailed structural reconstruction of the alleged programme was published not by any newsroom but on a YouTube channel; and the mainstream broadcast contribution came from a single correspondent at a fourth-place cable network. The consequences cut both ways. Long-form independent media gave the structural argument the room it needed - three and four hours, with timestamped chapter indexes that make claims retrievable and dateable - which no broadcast format would have allowed. It also removed the editorial layer that would have forced sourcing discipline, and it attached the whole enterprise to advertising, membership and merchandise revenue models that reward escalation. Both facts belong in any assessment of this record.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 825 of 1082, score 19). 10 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A0/D3.

Source: Wikipedia entries for Leslie Kean and Ralph Blumenthal; The Debrief, 5 June 2023, DEMOTED from Tier 1 (cited to a secondary/encyclopedic source); needs primary replacement
- **Tier 3 stated inference, allegation shaped wording as stored.** The commercial structure of independent long-form media - sponsorship reads, membership tiers, merchandise and tour revenue attached to episodes making structural allegations - creates an escalation incentive that must be weighed against the format's genuine advantage in allowing claims to be laid out at length and indexed. Source: Inference from the monetisation disclosed in the episode descriptions themselves.

## Program structure and organisational mapping

Program structure and organisational mapping is a domain on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2023; axes C0 A0 D3; 10 connections.

In one line: Research domain.

### What it is

The research domain concerned not with whether a legacy programme exists but with how it is organised: which offices hold it, which corporate forms contain it, which statutes shield it, which appropriations fund it and which individuals sit at the gates. This is the domain UAP Gerb effectively created as a public discipline and that Jesse Michels and Ross Coulthart amplified. Its methodological signature is the use of open organisational records - office symbols, FFRDC charters, acquisition authorities, board memberships, corporate acquisition histories - as the skeleton, with witness testimony used only to populate roles that the skeleton already predicts. That inversion is what distinguishes it from classical ufology and is why it is harder to dismiss: the offices, the FFRDCs and the statutory authorities are all documented and real, and the contested claim concerns their contents rather than their existence.

### Why it holds its tier

## Kirtland Air Force Base and Albuquerque, New Mexico

Kirtland Air Force Base and Albuquerque, New Mexico is a location on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1941; axes C2 A3 D1; 10 connections.

In one line: 51,558-acre USAF installation, southeast Albuquerque; AFRL Directed Energy, AFNWC, Manzano, KUMMSC, Sandia.

### What it is

Kirtland Air Force Base occupies 51,558 acres in the southeast quadrant of Albuquerque, sharing runways with the Albuquerque International Sunport. It began as three private airfields, was taken over as Albuquerque Army Air Base in 1941, renamed Kirtland Army Air Field on 24 February 1942 for Col. Roy C. Kirtland, and became Kirtland Air Force Base on 13 January 1948 - the same day as Wright-Patterson. Its relevance is that it is the single densest concentration of nuclear-weapons and directed-energy infrastructure in the United States sitting inside a city: Sandia National Laboratories, descended from the Manhattan Project's Z Division, is adjacent; the Air Force Nuclear Weapons Center and the 377th Air Base Wing are hosted here; the Air Force Research Laboratory Directed Energy Directorate runs the laser and high-power microwave portfolio here; the Manzano Base weapons storage complex, tunnelled into the Manzano Mountains on the base's eastern edge, merged administratively with Kirtland on 1 July 1971; and the Kirtland Underground Munitions Maintenance and Storage Complex, activated in 1992, is described in the base's own literature as the largest nuclear weapons storage facility in the world, with more than 300,000 square feet underground. Kirtland is also the operational address of the best-documented domestic UAP-adjacent counterintelligence operation on the record. Albuquerque businessman Paul Bennewitz, whose company Thunder Scientific sat on the base perimeter and sold humidity sensors licensed from Sandia in 1966, began a self-funded study in July 1979, filmed lights over the Manzano storage area and recorded anomalous signals, contacted the base on 24 October 1980 and briefed Kirtland officials on 10 November 1980. What followed was a sustained feed of fabricated material through the Air Force Office of Special Investigations detachment here, later admitted in public by AFOSI special agent Richard C. Doty and by UFO researcher William Moore, who stated on 1 July 1989 that he had participated in an effort to push Bennewitz toward a breakdown by feeding him false information. Bennewitz was hospitalised in August 1988 and died on 23 June 2003. The Bennewitz operation is the board's clearest evidence that counterintelligence resources were deployed against a private citizen over UAP-adjacent observations at a nuclear site, and Kirtland is where it happened.

### The board's reading

The Bennewitz operation is the best-documented instance in the American record of counterintelligence resources being deployed against a private citizen over UAP-adjacent observations, and both principal participants have admitted it. William Moore stated publicly on 1 July 1989 that he had attempted to push Bennewitz into a mental breakdown by feeding him false information; AFOSI special agent Richard C. Doty has said he was tasked in the 1980s with hoaxing documents and feeding false information to UFO researchers including Bennewitz; and a 1992 article held in the CIA library names both men. The material fed to Bennewitz - underground bases, alien-government agreements, the Dulce mythos - became load-bearing folklore that has contaminated the field for four decades. The stimulus that started it, the lights and signals Bennewitz recorded over the Manzano nuclear weapons storage area in 1979-1980, has never received an official explanation.

## The FFRDC Seam

The FFRDC Seam is a domain on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1947; axes C3 A3 D2; 9 connections.

In one line: Structural domain - the legal space between government and industry.

### What it is

This is the mechanism the whole board turns on, and it is not a theory - it is written into the Federal Acquisition Regulation. A federally funded research and development center is a research organisation operated under contract by a private entity - a nonprofit, a university, or an industrial consortium - to meet a special long-term research need that, in the FAR's language, cannot be met as effectively by existing in-house or contractor resources. As of February 2026 there were 41 FFRDCs sponsored or co-sponsored by 12 federal agencies. Three features of FAR 35.017 create the seam. First, sponsors are directed to build long-term strategic relationships: initial contracts may run five years and are renewable in similar increments, expressly to give the FFRDC stability, continuity and the ability to attract high-quality personnel. Second, and decisively, the FFRDC 'has access to government and supplier data, including sensitive and proprietary information, and to employees and facilities beyond that common in a normal contractual relationship.' That single clause creates an entity that can legally see both the government's compartmented information and the prime contractor's proprietary information - a vantage no ordinary contractor and no ordinary civil servant has. Third, in exchange for that access, the FFRDC is barred from competing with the private sector for federal R&D work and is required to operate in the public interest with objectivity, free from organisational conflicts of interest, and to fully disclose its activities to its sponsoring agency - to the sponsor, note, not to Congress and not to the public. The consequence is the concealment architecture in miniature. Records held by an FFRDC are contractor records, not agency records, and therefore outside the Freedom of Information Act. Disclosure runs to the sponsoring agency, which is also the classification authority. Sole-source awards mean no competitor ever audits the work. Personnel move continuously between the sponsor and the operator. If a programme wanted to be technically vested outside the government while the government retained absolute control of materials, breakthroughs and data - and while remaining invisible to FOIA, to GAO, and to committee staff without the sponsor's consent - it would be built exactly here. Rep. Eric Burlison made this the explicit basis of his May 2026 inquiry: FFRDCs, he wrote, 'often receive special access to government facilities, data, sponsor requirements, and sensitive technical information that ordinary private contractors may not receive,' and 'cannot become private vaults for federal records.'

### The board's reading

How the seam actually works, mechanism by mechanism. (1) RECORDS STATUS. Documents created and held by an FFRDC operator are the contractor's records, not agency records. The Freedom of Information Act reaches agency records. A congressional committee can subpoena a contractor, but it must first know the contractor holds something - which is why Burlison's method of naming a file by its exact designation, as he did with AF-ATIC-FILM 03/52 at MIT Lincoln Laboratory, worked where a general request would not have. (2) DISCLOSURE DIRECTION. FAR 35.017 obliges the FFRDC to fully disclose its activities to its sponsoring agency. The sponsor is simultaneously the customer, the funder and the original classification authority. There is no obligation running to Congress, to GAO, to the public, or to any other agency. MITRE's answer to Burlison - that it would 'coordinate with the federal agencies responsible for the work' - is not evasion in the colloquial sense; it is the regulation operating exactly as written. (3) DUAL ACCESS. The FFRDC sees the government's compartmented information and the prime contractor's proprietary information. No civil servant has the second; no ordinary contractor has the first. That is the informational chokepoint the 2026 UAP Gerb investigation identifies as the 'missing link', and it exists whether or not any of that investigation's specific allegations are true. (4) NON-COMPETITION. Because the FFRDC is barred from competing with industry for federal R&D, no commercial rival ever has cause or standing to audit its work. (5) PERPETUITY. Five-year contracts renewable indefinitely, awarded sole-source, produce institutional relationships measured in decades - MITRE's DoD relationship is 68 years old, Battelle's Pacific Northwest contract dates to 1978, UT-Battelle's Oak Ridge contract runs to 2030. (6) PERSONNEL FLOW. The same executives move between sponsor and operator: a former NRO director chairs MITRE's board, a former PDDNI is its vice chair, a Battelle executive is its CEO, a former STRATCOM commander runs IDA, a former IARPA director runs RAND, and AARO's founding director went to Oak Ridge. Put these six together and you have an entity with the government's access, the private sector's records status, no auditor, no expiry and a shared talent pool with its own overseer. The board's position is not that this proves a concealed programme exists. It is that if one exists, this is where it lives, and that the burden of demonstrating otherwise sits with the institutions that built the structure.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 170 of 1082, score 47). 9 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C3/A3/D2.

- **Tier 1 documented.** Burlison's 22 May 2026 letter to MITRE rests explicitly on this structure: 'FFRDCs often receive special access to government facilities, data, sponsor requirements, and sensitive technical information that ordinary private contractors may not receive.' Source: Rep. Eric Burlison letter to MITRE, 22 May 2026, as quoted by MeriTalk, 29 May 2026
- **Tier 1 documented.** FAR 35.017 grants FFRDCs access to government and supplier data, including sensitive and proprietary information, and to employees and facilities beyond that common in a normal contractual relationship. Source: CRS Report R44629, 'Federally Funded Research and Development Centers (FFRDCs): Background and Issues for Congress'
- **Tier 1 documented.** FFRDCs must operate in the public interest with objectivity and independence, be free from organisational conflicts of interest, fully disclose their activities to their sponsoring agency, and may not use their special access to compete with the private sector for federal R&D contracts. Source: CRS Report R44629, summarising FAR 35.017
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** The combination of contractor-held records outside FOIA, disclosure obligations running only to the sponsoring agency, and permanent sole-source relationships makes the FFRDC layer the single most defensible place in the US system to host an unacknowledged programme. Reasoning: each of the three properties independently removes an oversight mechanism, and no other institutional form removes all three simultaneously. Source: Structural inference from FAR 35.017 and the FOIA definition of agency records

## Enacted Statute

Enacted Statute is a domain on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2021-12-27; axes C0 A1 D3; 9 connections.

In one line: Legal domain - the provisions that actually became binding law (alias of d-legislative for enacted text).

### What it is

This domain isolates what is enacted from what was merely proposed, because the difference is the whole of the disclosure movement's leverage. The binding corpus is small and can be listed: the FY2022 NDAA provision that stood up the Airborne Object Identification and Management Synchronization Group and its successor office; the FY2023 NDAA language establishing AARO's statutory mandate and a secure reporting mechanism with protection against reprisal for current and former personnel and contractors; and Subtitle C of the FY2024 NDAA (P.L. 118-31), Sections 1841 through 1843, creating the UAP Records Collection at NARA with an affirmative agency duty to transmit and defined grounds for postponement. Everything else - the Review Board, eminent domain over recovered material, contractor amnesty, standalone whistleblower protection - remains proposed. The practical consequence is that the executive branch currently owes Congress reports and owes NARA records, but owes no independent body an answer, and no statute yet compels the surrender of a physical object. Scoring high on this domain means an entity is named in, administers, or is bound by enacted text.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 601 of 1082, score 27). 9 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A1/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** P.L. 118-159 Section 6802 sunsets requirements relating to audits of the UAP historical record report. Source: P.L. 118-159
- **Tier 1 documented.** P.L. 118-31, Subtitle C, Sections 1841-1843 are the enacted UAP records provisions: collection at NARA, agency review and transmission duty, and grounds for postponement. Source: P.L. 118-31
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena Disclosure Act's Review Board and eminent domain provisions passed the Senate as Division I of H.R. 2670 but were not enacted. Source: H.R. 2670 (EAS) compared with P.L. 118-31

### Its strongest connections

- Enacted Statute comes in from **10 U.S.C. 119, Special Access Programs: Congressional Oversight** (legislation, board tier A), a legislative link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: codified permanent law - the only statute governing what Congress is told about special access programs. Added by sec. 1132(a)(1) of the NDAA for FY1988-89, P.L. 100-180, signed 4 December 1987, and amended five times since. It is positive law in title 10 rather than a standalone provision, which places it at the centre of the enacted corpus this domain isolates. ## Legislative Process (proposed and pending)

Legislative Process (proposed and pending) is a domain on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2021-12-27; axes C0 A1 D3; 9 connections.

In one line: Bills, amendments and the actors that move them. Deliberately distinct from d-legislation (Enacted Statute): the gap between what passed the Senate and what survived conference is one of this board's central substantive findings, and collapsing the two would erase it.

### What it is

Legislation is where the disclosure fight has produced its only binding outcomes and its most instructive defeats, and the mechanism of the defeats matters more than their fact. The UAP Disclosure Act of 2023, carried as Division I of the Senate-passed FY2024 NDAA with ten sections, would have created an independent Review Board with authority to compel release and an eminent domain provision over recovered technologies of unknown origin held by any person. What emerged from conference and became law in P.L. 118-31 was Subtitle C - a records collection at NARA, a duty to transmit, and postponement grounds - with the Review Board and eminent domain removed. The removal happened in conference, where authorship is diffuse and no recorded vote attaches, which is the characteristic signature of this domain: UAP provisions rarely lose on the floor, they lose in the seam between chambers. The fight continued through the FY2025 NDAA's Section 6802 sunset of the historical-record audit requirements and into the FY2026 and FY2027 cycles. Scoring high on this domain means an entity drafts, sponsors, amends or strips this language - and stripping counts as much as sponsoring.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 533 of 1082, score 29). 9 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A1/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Division I of H.R. 2670 as passed by the Senate on 27 July 2023 was the 'Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena Disclosure Act of 2023', Sections 9001-9010, including a Review Board and eminent domain over recovered technologies of unknown origin. Source: H.R. 2670 (EAS), Division I
- **Tier 1 documented.** NDAA FY2025 (P.L. 118-159) Section 6802 is titled 'Sunset of requirements relating to audits of unidentified anomalous phenomena historical record report'. Source: P.L. 118-159, Sec. 6802
- **Tier 1 documented.** The enacted FY2024 NDAA (P.L. 118-31) contains Subtitle C, Sections 1841-1843, establishing the UAP Records Collection at NARA without the Review Board or eminent domain provisions. Source: P.L. 118-31
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The dilution of the Schumer-Rounds disclosure language during the FY2024 conference was reported contemporaneously as a deliberate weakening rather than a drafting accident. Source: The Debrief, 'A Dramatic Dilution', 22 December 2023

The link reads: passed one chamber, stripped in conference - the domain's instructive defeat. The only text ever passed by a chamber of Congress legislating on the assumption that recovered non-human technology exists and is held in part by private entities. It rode to the Senate floor as an amendment, cleared the Senate as a division of S. 2226, and did not survive conference into P.L. 118-31 in that form. The mechanism of the defeat is the lesson, which is why it belongs to process rather than to statute. ## Official Denial

Official Denial is a domain on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 1947-01-01; axes C0 A2 D2; 8 connections.

In one line: Evidentiary domain - the denial as a dated, attributable artifact.

### What it is

A denial is not the end of an inquiry on this board; it is an entry in it. The editorial rule is that every official denial gets recorded in full, with its author, its date, its precise wording and its scope - because the wording and the scope are the data. AARO's Volume I historical record report of February 2024 states flatly that the office found 'no empirical evidence' that the US government or private companies have reverse-engineered extraterrestrial technology; what the same report also states is the scope of its search, the access it was granted, and which named programmes it did and did not resolve, and those are the parts a hostile audit turns on. The documented pattern that makes this a domain rather than a footnote is the historical one: the National Reconnaissance Office's very existence was an official secret from 1961 until 1992, meaning that for thirty-one years an accurate answer to 'does that organisation exist' was legally required to be no. Denials therefore have a known failure mode - a categorical denial by an official without the relevant read-in is not a statement about the world but a statement about that official's access. Scoring on this domain measures how central official denial has been to an entity's public conduct: an agency spokesman who issues denials, a director who signs them, or a witness whose testimony a denial was written to rebut.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 109 of 1082, score 58). 8 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 8 official denials recorded against it. Axes C0/A2/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** AARO's Volume I historical record report (February 2024) concludes there is 'no empirical evidence' for claims that the USG and private companies have been reverse-engineering extraterrestrial technology. Source: AARO Historical Record Report Volume I, Section II
- **Tier 1 documented.** The existence of the National Reconnaissance Office was classified from its 1961 establishment until its declassification in September 1992, making official denial of its existence the required answer for thirty-one years. Source: NRO declassification record; FAS Intelligence Resource Program
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Air Force officers gave explanations to the public they knew to be false in order to protect U-2 and OXCART flight security, per CIA's own in-house history. Source: Haines, Studies in Intelligence (1997)

### Its strongest connections

- Official Denial comes in from **The Cover-Up** (domain, board tier A), a denial link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: Denial is the public face of concealment. CIA's own historian records that false explanations were issued knowingly to protect classified overflights; the denial is the output of the concealment activity. Sources: Haines, 'CIA's Role in the Study of UFOs, 1947-1990', Studies in Intelligence, https://www.cia.gov/resources/csi/studies-in-intelligence/studies-in-intelligence-1997/cias-role-in-the-study-of-ufos-1947-1990/, dated 1997-01-01.

The link reads: The Rockefeller Commission anonymised him and footnoted his stability. The published report calls him only an employee of the Department of the Army and attaches a footnote saying the few remaining Agency records indicate he may have had a history of emotional instability. Sources: Rockefeller Commission report, June 1975, p. 227, https://documents2.theblackvault.com/documents/rockefellercommissionreport-full.pdf, dated 1975-06.
- Official Denial comes in from **TR-AC-47, Joint ATIC-WADC Report on Project Silver Bug, 15 February 1955** (document, board tier B), a denial link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: A dated Air Force document dismissing the flying-saucer resemblance of an aircraft it was itself funding. The Summary states that the project 'should in no way be associated with any science fiction or "Flying Saucer" stories because of its external appearance', which is a denial with an author, a date and a subject, and is what this board's denial domain exists to hold. - Official Denial comes in from **CIA Prepublication Classification Review Board** (institution, board tier B), a denial link at Tier 1 documented. The mandatory disclaimer required on every approved writing states that nothing in it implies US Government authentication of the information, which is a formal, self-documented denial attached in advance to every cleared account. ## The HASC/HAC-D special access program read-in gate

The HASC/HAC-D special access program read-in gate is a domain on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2023; axes C0 A3 D3; 8 connections.

In one line: Access-control mechanism blocking Oversight.

### What it is

The specific bureaucratic mechanism by which the committee with subpoena power is kept away from the material it is investigating, stated on the record by the chair of the House body investigating it. On 9 September 2025 Anna Paulina Luna told the hearing that in preparation for previous UAP hearings the committee repeatedly asked the Department of Defense to allow members to view UAP videos and files, and that DoD notified committee staff that under the Department's special access program rules only members of the House Armed Services Committee and the Defense Subcommittee of House Appropriations could be read in on such programmes; for any non-committee member to view the documents and videos, individual members must be approved by the chairman AND ranking member of BOTH HASC and HAC-D. Luna added that independent staff oversight is a consistent problem because programme budgets are classified and oversight reporting to Congress goes only to the authorising and appropriating committees of jurisdiction. This is the operative architecture: it is not a refusal, it is a jurisdictional routing that makes refusal unnecessary. Eric Burlison supplied two further instances of the same pattern in the same hearing - an NDAA UAP disclosure amendment ruled non-germane 'mostly deemed by staff, not even an elected official,' and members blocked in 2024 from a full AARO briefing by 'someone in House administration.' The Holman Rule threat Ogles made in 2023 and the subpoena Gaetz asked for in 2023 and Luna raised in 2026 are the only instruments that route around this gate, and none has been used.

### The board's reading

The full text of the mechanism as stated by Chairwoman Luna on 9 September 2025: 'A major barrier to this Committee's inquiry into UAPs has been the lack of cooperation and transparency from the Department of Defense and the intelligence community. In preparation for previous UAP hearings, the Committee repeatedly asked the Department of Defense to allow members to view videos and files related to UAP incidences. Unfortunately, the Department of Defense notified the Committee staff that, due to the Department's special access program rules, only members of the House Armed Services Committee, as well as the Defense Subcommittee on House Appropriations, also known as HAC-D, were allowed to be read in onto such programs. For a non-committee member to be allowed to view these documents and videos, individual members must be approved by the Chairman and Ranking Member of both HASC and HAC-D. Independent staff oversight has presented a consistent problem for Congress, as well as program budgets are classified. Additionally, oversight reporting to Congress is classified and only provided to the authorizing and Appropriations Committees of jurisdiction.' Read alongside Burchett's July 2023 disclosure that members were denied a SCIF briefing before the founding hearing, Burlison's account of being blocked from a full AARO briefing by House Administration staff in 2024 and of his NDAA amendment being ruled non-germane by staff in 2025, and Gaetz's account of being refused the Eglin radar until he raised how authorities flow, the pattern is consistent: access is not refused on the merits, it is routed away on jurisdiction and procedure.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 326 of 1082, score 36). 8 connections, 1 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A3/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** 'Independent staff oversight has presented a consistent problem for Congress, as well as program budgets are classified. Additionally, oversight reporting to Congress is classified and only provided to the authorizing and Appropriations Committees of jurisdiction.' Source: CHRG-119hhrg61718, 9 September 2025
- **Tier 1 documented.** 'The Department of Defense notified the Committee staff that, due to the Department's special access program rules, only members of the House Armed Services Committee, as well as the Defense Subcommittee on House Appropriations, also known as HAC-D, were allowed to be read in onto such programs. For a non-committee member to be allowed to view these documents and videos, individual members must be approved by the Chairman and Ranking Member of both HASC and HAC-D.' Source: Chairwoman Anna Paulina Luna, 9 September 2025, GPO CHRG-119hhrg61718
- **Tier 1 documented.** Burlison, same hearing: his NDAA UAP disclosure amendment was ruled non-germane 'mostly deemed by staff, not even an elected official,' and in 2024 members were 'blocked by someone in House administration from being able to receive a full briefing from AARO.' Source: CHRG-119hhrg61718, 9 September 2025
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** The gate is jurisdictional rather than discretionary, which is what makes it durable: no official has to deny anything, because the routing rule disqualifies the requester. Reasoning: DoD's answer to Oversight was not 'no' but 'not your committee,' and the appeal route requires the assent of four separate members across two other committees. Source: Inference from Luna's 9 September 2025 statement

## Congressional Oversight

Congressional Oversight is a domain on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 1966-04-05; axes C0 A2 D3; 7 connections.

In one line: Institutional domain - the committees, clearances and gaps through which Congress does or does not see.

### What it is

Congressional oversight of the deepest access tiers is not a matter of will but of architecture, and the architecture is written down. Under 10 U.S.C. 119 the Secretary of Defense may waive notification of a special access programme so that only the chairs and ranking members of four defence committees are told - eight members, ordinarily without cleared staff, ordinarily orally, with no ability to take notes away or consult a colleague. A member outside that eight who suspects a programme exists has no lawful route to confirm it, and a member inside it has no lawful route to tell anyone. That is the structural fact the disclosure fight has been trying to break since 2017. The instruments Congress has reached for are traceable: mandatory reporting requirements in successive NDAAs, the creation of AARO with a statutory mandate to review the historical record, the Department of Defense Inspector General's evaluation DODIG-2023-109, and - on 9 September 2025 - a House Oversight hearing titled 'Restoring Public Trust Through UAP Transparency and Whistleblower Protection' that put the access problem itself on the record. Scoring high on this domain means an entity sits at the point where the executive's information meets Congress's right to it: a committee chair, a cleared staff director, an IG, or a member who forced a vote.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 550 of 1082, score 28). 7 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A2/D3.

## Skinwalker Ranch, Uintah Basin, Utah

Skinwalker Ranch, Uintah Basin, Utah is a location on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 1996; axes C3 A2 D2; 7 connections.

In one line: 512-acre property; NIDS 1996-2004 and AAWSAP field site 2008-2012.

### What it is

Skinwalker Ranch is a property of approximately 512 acres in Uintah County, Utah, southeast of Ballard and bordering the Uintah and Ouray Indian Reservation, at roughly 40 degrees 15 minutes north, 109 degrees 53 minutes west. Kenneth and Edith Myers held it from 1934 to 1994; Terry and Gwen Sherman bought it in 1994 and sold it in 1996; Robert Bigelow bought it for $200,000 in 1996 and sold it for about $500,000 in 2016 to Brandon Fugal, who announced his ownership through the Delaware entity Adamantium Real Estate LLC in March 2020. Its importance to this board is entirely institutional. Under Bigelow the property became the field site of the National Institute for Discovery Science, whose investigators included retired Army Col. John B. Alexander and biochemist Colm A. Kelleher and who logged close to a hundred incidents while conceding they could not obtain evidence to a publishable standard. The line that runs from this ranch into the federal government is documented and is the reason the location matters: DIA official Dr. James Lacatski read Kelleher and George Knapp's 2005 book 'Hunt for the Skinwalker', contacted Bigelow, visited the ranch, and reported an experience there; Bigelow relayed this to Sen. Harry Reid; Reid, with Sens. Ted Stevens and Daniel Inouye, arranged roughly $22 million in Defense Department appropriations for what became the Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications Program. Bigelow's company BAASS won the AAWSAP contract, stood up around fifty full-time investigators with top secret clearances in a matter of months, ran for 27 months, produced more than three dozen preliminary reports and over a hundred detailed papers, and built a data warehouse of more than 240,000 case files designed by Jacques Vallee that Knapp testified in September 2025 remains in use by US agencies. A private ranch in eastern Utah is therefore the physical origin point of the largest US government UAP investigation of the modern era, and the majority of what that investigation produced has never been released by DIA.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 88 of 1082, score 65). 9 connections, 4 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C3/A2/D2.

## The Cover-Up

The Cover-Up is a domain on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 1947-01-01; axes C2 A3 D1; 6 connections.

In one line: Operational domain - concealment run as a funded programme, not a passive silence.

### What it is

This board treats concealment as a discipline with doctrine, staffing and a budget line, because that is how the surviving record describes it. The 17 January 1953 Robertson Panel, convened by CIA's Office of Scientific Intelligence, did not merely conclude that sightings were harmless - it recommended that the national security agencies 'take immediate steps to strip the Unidentified Flying Objects of the special status they have been given and the aura of mystery they have unfortunately acquired', through a programme of mass-media debunking and the monitoring of civilian UFO groups. That is a covert influence recommendation written by an intelligence agency about its own public. Forty-four years later CIA historian Gerald K. Haines, writing in the agency's own classified-then-declassified journal Studies in Intelligence, admitted that Air Force officers had knowingly issued false explanations - natural phenomena, ice crystals, temperature inversions - to Congress and the public for sightings the Air Force knew were U-2 and OXCART overflights, and called this 'not the full story'. The documented route from ordinary secrecy into active concealment therefore runs through three mechanisms this board tracks separately: false official explanation, record destruction, and the redefinition of a programme so that the honest answer to a congressional question becomes technically 'no such programme exists'. GAO's 1995 search for Roswell records found that outgoing messages from Roswell Army Air Field for 1946-1949 had been destroyed with no disposition authority and no destruction certificate on file - a documented gap where the destruction itself is the evidence. To be scored highly on this domain is to be a producer of concealment rather than merely a person kept in the dark by it: an author of cover stories, a signatory on a destruction order, a briefer who gave a denial he knew to be false.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 141 of 1082, score 52). 6 connections, 3 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 2 official denials recorded against it. Axes C2/A3/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** CIA historian Gerald K. Haines, in Studies in Intelligence (1997), states that over half of all UFO reports in the late 1950s and 1960s were accounted for by U-2 and OXCART flights and that Air Force investigators knowingly provided false explanations to the public. Source: Haines, 'CIA's Role in the Study of UFOs, 1947-1990', Studies in Intelligence
- **Tier 1 documented.** GAO (NSIAD-95-187, 28 July 1995) reported that Roswell Army Air Field outgoing messages for 1946-1949 were destroyed without a disposition authority or destruction certificate. Source: GAO/NSIAD-95-187, Government Records: Results of a Search for Records Concerning the 1947 Crash Near Roswell, New Mexico
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Robertson Panel report (17 January 1953) recommends stripping UFOs of their 'special status' through mass-media debunking and recommends that civilian UFO groups be 'watched because of their potentially great influence on mass thinking'. Source: Robertson Panel report, declassified text, CUFON
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** A concealment effort sustained across eight decades and multiple agencies requires standing institutional machinery - security classification guides, cover-story doctrine, and personnel whose job is the maintenance of the cover - rather than repeated ad hoc decisions. Reasoning: the documented 1953 recommendation, the documented 1950s-60s false-explanation practice and the documented 1990s record gaps span three distinct institutional generations with no continuity of individual actors. Source: Inference from the Robertson Panel report, the Haines article and GAO/NSIAD-95-187

## Freedom of Information Practice

Freedom of Information Practice is a domain on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1967-07-04; axes C0 A0 D3; 6 connections.

In one line: Procedural domain - the request, the appeal, the Glomar, and what survives the exemptions.

### What it is

The Freedom of Information Act is the only routine instrument by which a private citizen can compel a federal agency to produce a record, and almost every primary document on this board arrived through it. Its limits are as instructive as its yield. Exemption 1 shields properly classified national defence information; Exemption 3 shields information withheld by other statute - including the National Security Agency Act, which NSA has successfully invoked to refuse even to describe its UFO-related holdings; and the Glomar response permits an agency to refuse to confirm or deny that responsive records exist at all. In Citizens Against UFO Secrecy v. NSA the courts sustained NSA's withholding of the overwhelming majority of its UFO-related documents on the strength of the Yeates affidavit, a filing whose own redacted form is a documented artifact of the concealment. The structural gap this board turns on is that FFRDC and prime-contractor records are contractor records, not agency records, and are outside FOIA entirely - so material that moves from a government shelf to a contractor shelf moves out of the statute's reach without ever being classified higher. Scoring high on this domain means an entity litigates, processes, or is the subject of these requests.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 504 of 1082, score 30). 6 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 2 official denials recorded against it. Axes C0/A0/D3.

- **Tier 1 documented.** NSA's released UFO document collection, including the Yeates affidavit and associated litigation records, is publicly archived. Source: The Black Vault, UFOs: The National Security Agency (NSA) Collection
- **Tier 1 documented.** The DIA released the AAWSAP contract file under FOIA case FOIA-00349-2018, producing the statement of work, contract modifications and the Defense Intelligence Reference Documents. Source: DIA FOIA release FOIA-00349-2018
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** Records held by contractors and FFRDCs are outside FOIA's definition of agency records, so transferring material into contractor custody removes it from the statute without any change in classification. Reasoning: FOIA reaches agency records; FAR 35.017 FFRDC records are held by the operating contractor. Source: Inference from FOIA's agency-records requirement and FAR 35.017

## Psychological Operations & Perception Management

Psychological Operations & Perception Management is a domain on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1953-01-17; axes C1 A2 D1; 6 connections.

In one line: Operational domain - deliberate manipulation of belief as a security measure.

### What it is

The use of the UFO subject as a vehicle for deception against a domestic audience is documented at the level of an official recommendation and at the level of a named case. The recommendation is the Robertson Panel's January 1953 proposal for a debunking campaign using mass media, psychiatrists and advertising specialists, explicitly aimed at reducing public susceptibility rather than at establishing what the objects were. The named case is Paul Bennewitz, an Albuquerque physicist living beside Kirtland AFB, who in the early 1980s was fed fabricated documents and staged material by Air Force Office of Special Investigations personnel - most prominently Richard C. Doty, whose later New Mexico State Police personnel file was released under public records law and runs to 321 pages - with the documented effect of destroying his mental health. The dual-use route is exact: a genuine sensitive programme adjacent to a witness generates a security problem, and the cheapest solution is not to silence the witness but to discredit him by feeding him something absurd and letting him repeat it. That mechanism cuts in both directions and must be applied honestly - it explains real disinformation injected into the field, and it is also the most common frame used to dismiss testimony that is not disinformation. Scoring high here means an entity ran, authored or knowingly transmitted deception material.

## Records & Archives

Records & Archives is a domain on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1992-10-26; axes C1 A1 D3; 6 connections.

In one line: Legal-procedural domain - the statutory fight over custody, transfer and destruction of the paper.

### What it is

Whoever controls the records controls the history, and this is the one front on which the disclosure movement has produced enacted law. Subtitle C of the FY2024 National Defense Authorization Act (P.L. 118-31) created, in Section 1841, an Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena Records Collection at the National Archives; in Section 1842, an affirmative duty on every government office to review, identify and transmit UAP records to NARA; and in Section 1843, grounds for postponement - a framework in which withholding is temporary and must be justified rather than permanent and unexplained. The model is the President John F. Kennedy Assassination Records Collection Act of 1992, which is why the Senate's original UAP Disclosure Act of 2023 also proposed an independent Review Board with the power to order release over agency objection; that Review Board and the eminent-domain provision over recovered material were stripped before enactment. What survived is a records statute without an independent adjudicator, which places the postponement decision back inside the agencies that hold the records. The stakes are set by the documented history: GAO found in 1995 that Roswell Army Air Field's outgoing messages for 1946-1949 had been destroyed with no destruction certificate. Scoring high on this domain means an entity holds, transfers, withholds or destroys records - or wrote the law governing those acts.

- **Tier 1 documented.** The President John F. ## The four SAP reporting regimes and the seams between them

The four SAP reporting regimes and the seams between them is a domain on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1987-12-04; axes C1 A3 D3; 6 connections.

In one line: analysis.

### What it is

There is no single United States statute on special access programs. There are four parallel regimes plus covert action, and the differences between them are where a program can live. (1) DoD - 10 U.S.C. 119: annual report by 1 March, new-program notice by 1 February, 14-day declassification notice, 30-day report-and-wait, all to six defense committees; the Secretary may waive the reports on a national-security determination, in which case the information goes only to the chairman and ranking minority member of each. (2) NNSA - 50 U.S.C. 2426: identical structure to the defense committees, but the Administrator may additionally waive the 30-day report-and-wait itself. (3) Every other agency - 50 U.S.C. 3348: identical structure, but reporting runs to 'congressional oversight committees' expressly including House Oversight and Senate Homeland Security, and the waiver does not narrow the audience. (4) The Intelligence Community - 50 U.S.C. 3091a and ICD 906: no annual statutory deadline and no waiver at all, but semiannual briefings on every program, a prohibition on establishment before notification, a funding cutoff since December 2024, and a prohibition on transferring capabilities out of a CAP into a SAP without notice since December 2024. Set beside each other, the ranking is unambiguous: the weakest reporting regime in the United States government belongs to the Department of Defense, and it is weakest precisely at the point where the Secretary decides how much to tell the committees. Two structural seams follow. The first is between regimes: a capability transferred from an IC controlled access program to a DoD special access program changes which statute governs it and which committees see it - the manoeuvre Congress prohibited prospectively in December 2024, three decades after the alleged conduct. The second is around all four: ICD 906 section C.3 records that none of them applies to 'special access programs under the purview of the National Security Council, such as covert action', which are governed only by 50 U.S.C. 3093 and its Gang of Eight.

### The board's reading

Where a program could sit, and what changes when it moves. There are five distinct legal environments, and the practical differences between them are the map of where concealment is easiest. (1) A DoD special access program: reports to six defense committees, waivable to eight to twelve members by the Secretary's own determination. (2) An NNSA special access program: same committees, and the Administrator may additionally waive the 30-day advance notice itself, so a program can begin with no committee ever having been told in advance - and NNSA material is Restricted Data, born classified by operation of the Atomic Energy Act rather than by an original classification authority, so a presidential declassification order operating on E.O. 13526 information does not reach it. (3) A non-DoD, non-NNSA, non-IC agency special access program: reports to House Oversight and Senate Homeland Security among others, and the waiver does not narrow the audience - the strongest of the SAP regimes, and the one covering DHS, NASA, Justice and Commerce. (4) An IC Controlled Access Program: no waiver at all, semiannual briefings, a funding cutoff since December 2024 and, since December 2024, a prohibition on transferring a capability out into a special access program without notice. (5) A National Security Council covert action program: outside all four SAP regimes by the express terms of ICD 906 section C.3, governed only by 50 U.S.C. 3093. The seam that matters is between (4) and (1). Before 23 December 2024 there was no statutory requirement to notify anyone when a capability moved from an IC controlled access program into a DoD special access program, and the move changes both the governing statute and the committees entitled to know. Congress closed that door prospectively in section 6603 of Public Law 118-159 - and left it ajar with an exception for transfers 'necessary to maintain access in the event of an organizational restructuring', a determination the transferring official makes for himself.

## Acknowledged, unacknowledged and waived, the three states of a DoD SAP

Acknowledged, unacknowledged and waived, the three states of a DoD SAP is a domain on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2010-07-01; axes C2 A3 D3; 6 connections.

In one line: taxonomy.

### What it is

The three-state taxonomy is the vocabulary the whole board depends on, and it is defined word for word in DoD's own issuances. An ACKNOWLEDGED SAP is 'A SAP whose existence is acknowledged, affirmed, or made known to others, but its specific details (technologies, materials, techniques, etc.,) are classified as specified in the applicable SCG'. Its name appears in the budget; you may know that it exists and nothing else. An UNACKNOWLEDGED SAP is 'A SAP having enhanced security measures ensuring the existence of the program is not acknowledged, affirmed, or made known to any person not authorized for such information'. The correct response to an inquiry is neither confirmation nor denial; the program's very existence is classified. A WAIVED SAP is 'A SAP for which the SecDef has waived applicable reporting in accordance with Reference (c) following a determination of adverse effect to national security. An unacknowledged SAP that has more restrictive reporting and access controls than other unacknowledged SAPs' - Reference (c) being 10 U.S.C. 119. Cross-cutting the three states are three categories defined by mission: an acquisition SAP protects 'sensitive research, development, testing and evaluation, modification, and procurement activities'; an intelligence SAP protects intelligence activity; and an operations and support SAP is 'established primarily to protect the planning for, execution of, and support to especially sensitive military operations' and 'may protect organizations, property, operational concepts, plans, or activities'. A SAP with subordinate structure is called an 'umbrella', with 'compartments, sub-compartments, or projects' beneath it. The single most important observation for this board is that the alleged legacy recovery effort - if it exists - would sit precisely at the intersection of a waived unacknowledged operations and support SAP with an acquisition SAP for the exploitation work, held under an umbrella whose compartments are known only to the compartment holders. Every element of that description is an ordinary term of art in DoDI 5205.11.

### The board's reading

Terms of art, precisely, and why each one matters to the allegations. ACKNOWLEDGED: existence admitted, details classified. If a recovery program were acknowledged, its nickname would be in the annual report and the argument would be over. UNACKNOWLEDGED: 'enhanced security measures ensuring the existence of the program is not acknowledged, affirmed, or made known to any person not authorized'. This is the state in which a truthful official can decline to confirm or deny, and in which a denial issued by a press office staffed by people without access is a denial made in good faith by people who genuinely do not know. WAIVED: an unacknowledged SAP whose statutory reporting has been removed by the Secretary. This is the state in which the phrase 'Congress was not informed' can be simultaneously true and lawful. Cross-cutting these are the mission categories - acquisition, intelligence, and operations and support, the last defined to protect 'the planning for, execution of, and support to especially sensitive military operations' and expressly capable of protecting 'organizations, property, operational concepts, plans, or activities'. The word 'property' in an official DoD definition is the closest thing in the public policy corpus to a category designed for custody of a physical object whose existence is itself the secret. And any SAP with subordinate structure is an 'umbrella' with 'compartments, sub-compartments, or projects' - which is why a person read into one compartment can truthfully testify to what he was told about others without ever having seen a document.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 61 of 1082, score 73). 6 connections, 4 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C2/A3/D3.

## The Aerospace Corporation

The Aerospace Corporation is a institution on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1960; axes C2 A3 D1; 5 connections.

In one line: Nonprofit operator of the Aerospace FFRDC for the US Space Force; El Segundo, CA; founded 1960.

### What it is

Aerospace is the FFRDC that owns the technical judgement on everything the United States puts into, or detects from, orbit - and it is the second named target of the congressional inquiry. It was created in 1960 at the Air Force's insistence, announced by Lt. Gen. Bernard Schriever, to provide dedicated scientific and technical planning for the military space and ballistic missile programmes after the conflict-of-interest problems of the earlier Ramo-Wooldridge arrangement. Congress's Office of Technology Assessment noted in its 1995 history of DoD FFRDCs that the Air Force insisted on specific restrictions in the Aerospace charter that it had not imposed elsewhere - the government designed this one to be structurally constrained precisely because it would sit at the centre of national security space. Today Aerospace operates the only FFRDC committed exclusively to the space enterprise, with roughly 4,800 employees, funded at approximately 1,309.8 million dollars in FY2024, under Space Force contract FA880219C0001 recorded on USAspending at approximately 8.67 billion dollars over 2018-2028. Its relevance to this board is direct: any persistent orbital or transmedium object detected by national security space sensors would be characterised technically by Aerospace, not by the Space Force in-house, and Aerospace also performs system engineering and integration for NNSA's Defense Nuclear Nonproliferation and Defense Programs offices under DOE contract 89233120CNA000134. Rep. Burlison has named Aerospace explicitly among the organisations his investigation is following, and Ross Coulthart has publicly raised Aerospace and MITRE in connection with the 2026 UAP Governance Board. No document has been produced placing Aerospace in a legacy programme. What is documented is that it holds the technical keys to the space sensor picture and that Congress has now put it on the list.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 190 of 1082, score 45). 5 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A3/D1.

- **Tier 1 documented.** The Office of Technology Assessment's 1995 history records that the Air Force specifically insisted on restrictions in the Aerospace Corporation charter that it had not imposed on earlier FFRDCs.
