## Armed Forces Special Weapons Project

Armed Forces Special Weapons Project is a institution on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1946-12-31 to 1959-05-06; axes C2 A3 D1; 5 connections.

In one line: Joint Army-Navy atomic energy organisation, 1946-1959; military successor to the Manhattan Engineer District.

### What it is

The Armed Forces Special Weapons Project was established by joint directive of the Secretaries of War and the Navy, effective midnight 31 December 1946, to carry on the military service functions of the Manhattan Project that were retained under armed forces control when the Atomic Energy Commission took the civil ones. It reported directly to the Army Chief of Staff and the Chief of Naval Operations, its Chief and Deputy Chief sat on the Military Liaison Committee to the AEC, and its funds came by working fund advances from the AEC out of money formerly available to the Manhattan Project. Major General Leslie R. Groves became its first Chief on 28 February 1947. It was redesignated the Defense Atomic Support Agency in 1959 and, through DNA and DSWA, is the ancestor of the Defense Threat Reduction Agency.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 168 of 1082, score 47). 5 connections, 1 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A3/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** AFSWP took the military half of the Manhattan Project. The charter makes it 'responsible for carrying on the military service functions of the Manhattan Project retained under control of the armed forces', including military participation in the development of atomic weapons of all types in coordination with the Atomic Energy Commission, and assigns to it as of 31 December 1946 the Army units that had been assigned to the Manhattan Engineer District. Source: War Department Memorandum No. 850-25-8, paras. 2, 3 and 5, in Defense's Nuclear Agency 1947-1997, Appendix D, https://archive.org/download/DTIC_ADA412977/DTIC_ADA412977_djvu.txt
- **Tier 1 documented.** Groves held the command for one year to the day. The Defense Threat Reduction Agency's chronology of its own lineage reads: 'Feb. 28, 1947. Maj Gen Groves becomes first Chief AFSWP, serving for one year, until his retirement on February 29, 1948; he is promoted to Lt. General on January 24, 1948.' The same history records that the command stood empty when it was created: 'between the beginning of January and the end of February 1947, there was no officially appointed chief of AFSWP. During this period, Groves' aide, Colonel Hasbrouck, assumed Headquarters command in Washington as the senior officer of the organization.' Source: Defense's Nuclear Agency 1947-1997, Defense Threat Reduction Agency, 2002, Appendix C: Chronology, DTIC ADA412977, https://archive.org/download/DTIC_ADA412977/DTIC_ADA412977_djvu.txt
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Atomic Energy Commission funded the military organisation. Paragraph 6 of the charter: funds for AFSWP other than military pay 'have been made available by working fund advances to War Department from the Atomic Energy Commission, utilizing funds formerly available to the Manhattan Project.' Source: War Department Memorandum No. 850-25-8, para. 6, in Defense's Nuclear Agency 1947-1997, Appendix D, https://archive.org/download/DTIC_ADA412977/DTIC_ADA412977_djvu.txt
- **Tier 1 documented.** The National Archives states the same establishment independently: 'AFSWP established as an interservice agency, effective December 31, 1946, by a joint letter of the Secretaries of War and the Navy, January 29, 1947, with responsibility for discharging all military functions relating to atomic energy in coordination with the Atomic Energy Commission'. Source: Records of the Defense Threat Reduction Agency, Record Group 374, sec. 374.2, Guide to Federal Records in the National Archives, https://www.archives.gov/research/guide-fed-records/groups/374.html
- **Tier 1 documented.** The charter is reproduced in the Department of Defense's own agency history: 'The Armed Forces Special Weapons Project established by joint directive of the Secretaries of War and Navy, effective as of midnight 31 December 1946, will operate directly under the Chief of Staff and the Chief of Naval Operations.' Source: War Department Memorandum No. 850-25-8, 18 March 1947, reproduced in Appendix D of Defense's Nuclear Agency 1947-1997, Defense Threat Reduction Agency, 2002, DTIC ADA412977, https://archive.org/download/DTIC_ADA412977/DTIC_ADA412977_djvu.txt
- **Tier 1 documented.** The founding memorandum was signed by Secretary of War Robert P. Patterson and Secretary of the Navy James V. Forrestal on 29 January 1947, and it directed that both the head of AFSWP and his deputy serve as members of the Military Liaison Committee to the AEC. Major General Leslie R. Groves became first Chief on 28 February 1947 and served one year to his retirement on 29 February 1948. Source: Defense's Nuclear Agency 1947-1997, ch. 'The Post-War Transition, 1946 to 1948', p. 30, and Appendix C chronology, https://archive.org/download/DTIC_ADA412977/DTIC_ADA412977_djvu.txt
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** On Debriefed, episode 80, 20 March 2026, the guest Gerb said AFSWP 'was formed in between 1947 and 1948 at Los Alamos Z division which of course is San Dia base' and was 'a founded joint venture under it was the Navy and some element of IC', 'founded under James Forestall'. The opened record contradicts the date, the founding authority and the composition: it was effective 31 December 1946, it was an Army-Navy organisation with no intelligence component in its charter, and Forrestal signed jointly with the Secretary of War. Source: Batch 17 file 03, 1:28;19-1:29;13, research/leads/area52-corpus-17.md; contradicted by War Department Memorandum No. 850-25-8, https://archive.org/download/DTIC_ADA412977/DTIC_ADA412977_djvu.txt

## Bolender Memorandum

Bolender Memorandum is a document on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1969; axes C1 A3 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: Brig. Gen. C. H. Bolender, 20 October 1969.

### What it is

The memorandum by Brig. Gen. Carroll H. Bolender, USAF Deputy Director of Development, dated 20 October 1969, recommending termination of Project Blue Book. Its historical importance is not the recommendation but the justification. Bolender explains that reports of unidentified flying objects which could affect national security are made in accordance with JANAP 146 or Air Force Manual 55-11 and are not part of the Blue Book system, and therefore that terminating Blue Book would not affect the Air Force's ability to handle national-security cases. That is an official written statement that the program the public believed was the government's UFO investigation had never handled the cases that mattered. The memo also observes that the retention of Blue Book records would not be a problem once the project ended, and Blue Book's holdings duly went to the National Archives while whatever accumulated on the JANAP side has never been identified as a records series, never been accessioned, and never been searched. Two months after Bolender wrote, Secretary of the Air Force Robert C. Seamans Jr. announced Blue Book's closure on 17 December 1969, citing the Condon Report.

### The board's reading

The Bolender memorandum is short, administrative, and the most quietly damaging document in the legacy corpus. Its purpose was to justify closing Project Blue Book, and its argument was that closure would cost the Air Force nothing operationally because the reports that mattered had never been in Blue Book to begin with: UFO reports which could affect national security were made in accordance with JANAP 146 or Air Force Manual 55-11, and were not part of the Blue Book system. Read against JANAP 146(C) of 10 March 1954, which made unidentified flying objects a mandatory CIRVIS report and prohibited unauthorised disclosure of report contents under 18 U.S.C. 793 and 794, the memo describes a two-track structure operating for at least fifteen years: a public program that generated 12,618 catalogued sightings and a residue of 701 unknowns, and a classified channel whose output has never been named as a records series, never been accessioned by the National Archives, and never been searched under any disclosure statute, including the FY2024 NDAA provisions that created Record Group 615. Blue Book's records went to College Park. The other channel's records went somewhere that has not been identified in fifty-seven years. That gap is the specific, concrete, documentable thing this board exists to track, and it was opened by a brigadier general writing routine staff correspondence, not by a whistleblower.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 175 of 1082, score 46). 5 connections, 3 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C1/A3/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Blue Book was terminated on 17 December 1969, two months after the memorandum. Source: USAF Fact Sheet, 'Unidentified Flying Objects and Air Force Project Blue Book'
- **Tier 1 documented.** The memorandum is dated 20 October 1969 and signed by Brig. Gen. C. H. Bolender, USAF Deputy Director of Development. Source: Bolender memorandum, 20 Oct 1969 (archived scan)
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The memorandum states that reports of unidentified flying objects which could affect national security are made under JANAP 146 or AFM 55-11 and 'are not part of the Blue Book system'. Source: Bolender memorandum, 20 Oct 1969, as quoted throughout the literature; the archived scan is an untranscribed image
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** No records series corresponding to the JANAP 146 national-security UFO reports has ever been identified, accessioned by NARA, or searched under any subsequent disclosure statute. Source: Inference from the absence of any such series in NARA's Air Force UFO holdings description and in the RG 615 accessioning notices

## Capitol UAP disclosure event, 9 June 2026

Capitol UAP disclosure event, 9 June 2026 is a event on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2026; axes C1 A1 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: Lawmakers and whistleblowers, U.S. Capitol.

### What it is

On 9 June 2026 lawmakers and UAP whistleblowers gathered at the US Capitol to renew demands for transparency, with David Grusch returning to the Hill for the first time since his 2023 testimony. The specific asks were sharper than at any hearing: Anna Paulina Luna said lawmakers were requesting that the White House grant immunity to whistleblowers who come forward with information including the locations of UAP craft and advanced technology; Eric Burlison echoed the immunity call and asked President Trump to waive all non-disclosure agreements for UAP whistleblowers, while describing a 'Tom Clancy-style dead drop' of information to his office. Grusch, addressing the gatekeeping directly, said 'Political appointees have not complied with the disclosure law.' Moskowitz: 'Disclosure today, disclosure tomorrow. The American people deserve to know.' Burchett said a congressional tour of Area 51 would yield a T-shirt because the government would move everything else first. Lawmakers also called for the release of records relating to the 1996 Varginha incident in Brazil. The recurring theme was money and oversight rather than biology: whether the Pentagon is spending outside congressional reach.

## Conventional Prompt Global Strike

Conventional Prompt Global Strike is a program on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2003; axes C1 A1 D1; 5 connections.

In one line: Air Force, Navy and Army effort to place conventional warheads on long-range boosters and hypersonic glide vehicles, 2003 to present.

### What it is

Conventional Prompt Global Strike is the family of American programmes intended to strike a target anywhere on earth with a conventional warhead in under an hour, using long-range ballistic boosters and hypersonic glide vehicles. It is on this board for one reason: it is named on the Danny Jones podcast as the destination of reverse-engineered craft propulsion, and the Congressional Research Service report on it contradicts that. Every boost stage the report names is an existing American missile, the Trident II D-5, the three Peacekeeper stages of a Minotaur IV, and a STARS stack derived from Polaris, and the programme is funded in the open at hundreds of millions a year. The node is a contradiction node and its desc says so.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 307 of 1082, score 37). 5 connections, 2 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A1/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** It is funded on the visible budget: 'The Navy received $512 million for this program in FY2020 and requested $1.1 billion for FY2021; Congress appropriated $766.6 million for FY2021. The Navy has requested $1.4 billion for FY2022.' Source: CRS R41464, 16 July 2021, https://www.everycrsreport.com/reports/R41464.html
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Air Force glide vehicle flew on decommissioned ICBM stages: the third HTV-2 vehicle was launched on a 'Minotaur IV rocket, in its "lite" configuration, with only the three Peacekeeper stages.' Source: CRS R41464, 16 July 2021, https://www.everycrsreport.com/reports/R41464.html
- **Tier 1 documented.** The Army glide vehicle flew on a recycled submarine-missile stack: the Advanced Hypersonic Weapon 'used the strategic targets system (STARS) booster stack, which is derived from the Navy's Polaris ballistic missile.' Source: CRS R41464, 16 July 2021, https://www.everycrsreport.com/reports/R41464.html
- **Tier 1 documented.** The mission and the timescale: 'The United States sought to develop systems with intermediate or long range, so that they could attack targets around the world in under an hour.' The report adds that 'The Air Force and Navy have both pursued programs that would lead to the deployment of conventional warheads on their long-range ballistic missiles.' Source: Congressional Research Service, Conventional Prompt Global Strike and Long-Range Ballistic Missiles: Background and Issues, R41464, 16 July 2021, https://www.everycrsreport.com/reports/R41464.html
- **Tier 1 documented.** The programme has a documented start and a documented sponsor: 'In 2003, the Air Force and DARPA (the Defense Advanced Research Projects Agency) initiated a program, known as FALCON (force application and launch from continental United States).' Source: CRS R41464, 16 July 2021, https://www.everycrsreport.com/reports/R41464.html
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** On the Danny Jones Podcast, episode 224, 19 February 2024, between 3:03:15 and 3:04:55, Daniel Sheehan named Prompt Global Strike as an effort to use reverse-engineered craft propulsion for a two-minute missile strike into Russia or China, tied to the congressional districts containing Wright-Patterson Air Force Base and Redstone Arsenal. The CRS report contradicts both halves: it traces every booster to an existing American ballistic missile and states the goal as striking targets in under an hour. Source: Danny Jones Podcast, ep. 224, 19 February 2024, 3:03:15 to 3:04:55, mapped at research/leads/dannyjones-corpus-33.md

## Special Access Programs

Special Access Programs is a domain on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1960-01-01; axes C2 A3 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: Access-control domain - the SAP/USAP/waived tier and its oversight carve-outs.

### What it is

A special access programme is the mechanism by which the executive branch creates an information space with tighter controls than Top Secret and a smaller list of people who may enter it. The tier structure is documented policy, not speculation: acknowledged SAPs whose existence is public though their content is not; unacknowledged SAPs whose very existence is classified; and waived unacknowledged SAPs for which the Secretary of Defense has exercised the 10 U.S.C. 119 waiver so that notification runs only to the chairs and ranking members of the armed services and defence appropriations committees - eight members, typically without staff, typically orally. That last category is the load-bearing one for this board, because it is a lawful configuration in which a programme can run for decades while the overwhelming majority of Congress has no lawful means of learning it exists. In 2009 the Office of the Under Secretary of Defense for Intelligence prepared an information memorandum reviewing a request to convert DIA's advanced aerospace work into a special access programme - a documented instance of the transition being considered for exactly this subject matter. Scoring high on this domain means an entity held, granted, or was formally denied SAP-level access, and the denial is scored as heavily as the grant.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 235 of 1082, score 41). 5 connections, 2 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A3/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** 10 U.S.C. 119(e) permits waiver of SAP notification requirements, reducing reporting to the chairmen and ranking members of the defence committees. Source: 10 U.S.C. Sec. 119
- **Tier 1 documented.** An OUSD(I) information memorandum records a review of a special access program request relating to the Advanced Aerospace Threat Identification Program, dated November 2009. Source: DIA release, OUSDI Info Memo on AATIP (Review of Special Access Program Request), via The Black Vault
- **Tier 1 documented.** DoD Directive 5205.07 establishes acknowledged, unacknowledged and waived SAP categories and the associated reporting regimes. Source: DoD Directive 5205.07

## Secrecy Law & Classification

Secrecy Law & Classification is a domain on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1946-08-01; axes C1 A3 D1; 5 connections.

In one line: Legal domain - the statutory and executive machinery that makes concealment lawful.

### What it is

Everything this board describes is, on its face, legal - and that is the point. Classification authority flows from executive order rather than statute for most national security information, which means the same executive branch that runs a programme also decides whether Congress and the courts may learn of it. Layered on top are the Atomic Energy Act's 'born classified' Restricted Data regime, the Invention Secrecy Act's power to impose secrecy orders on patent applications, and 50 U.S.C. 3510's authority for unvouchered and confidential expenditure certified by agency heads rather than audited. The dual-use route into a concealment programme is simple and requires no conspiracy: an activity placed inside a special access programme, then waived from ordinary congressional notification under the exception in 10 U.S.C. 119, is protected by exactly the same machinery whether it holds a stealth aircraft or a recovered craft, and the machinery cannot tell the difference. Scoring high on this domain means an entity is a wielder of classification authority - an original classification authority, a security officer, a programme security manager - rather than a person subject to it.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 419 of 1082, score 32). 5 connections, 1 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A3/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** 10 U.S.C. 119 requires congressional notification of special access programmes but permits the Secretary of Defense to waive that notification for a programme, reporting instead only to the chairs and ranking members of the defence committees. Source: 10 U.S.C. Sec. 119, Special access programs: congressional oversight
- **Tier 1 documented.** 50 U.S.C. 3510 permits certain intelligence expenditures to be accounted for solely on the certificate of the agency head, with that certificate deemed a sufficient voucher. Source: 50 U.S.C. 3510 (Cornell LII)
- **Tier 1 documented.** DoD Directive 5205.07 establishes the policy framework for acknowledged, unacknowledged and waived special access programmes and assigns the approval authority. Source: DoD Directive 5205.07, Special Access Program (SAP) Policy

### Its strongest connections

- Secrecy Law & Classification runs out to **Special Access Programs** (domain, board tier B), a institutional link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: Statutory basis for the access tier. 10 U.S.C. 119 and DoDD 5205.07 are the legal and policy instruments that create the special access programme category; the SAP domain is the applied form of the secrecy-law domain. Sources: 10 U.S.C. Sec. 119, https://uscode.house.gov/view.xhtml?req=granuleid:USC-prelim-title10-section119&num=0&edition=prelim, dated 2024-01-01; DoD Directive 5205.07, https://www.esd.whs.mil/Portals/54/Documents/DD/issuances/dodd/520507p.pdf, dated 2024-09-12.

## Signals & Sensor Intelligence

Signals & Sensor Intelligence is a domain on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1952-11-04; axes C1 A3 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: Technical domain - radar, SIGINT, MASINT and the classified data that would settle the question.

### What it is

The data that could resolve most of this exists, and it is classified for reasons that have nothing to do with the objects. Radar tracks, infrared imagery, electronic intelligence collection and measurement and signature intelligence are protected because releasing them reveals sensor sensitivity, coverage geometry and collection technique - the classic sources-and-methods problem - which means the government can be entirely sincere in withholding material that would also settle a public controversy. The National Security Agency's posture is the documented extreme: in Citizens Against UFO Secrecy v. NSA the courts sustained withholding of the substance of the agency's UFO-related holdings on the strength of the classified Yeates affidavit, and the released material is heavily redacted. The institutional convergence became explicit in August 2024, when a senior NSA researcher, Jon Kosloski, was appointed to lead AARO - placing a signals-intelligence technologist at the head of the office responsible for the government's UAP analysis. Congress has pushed on the same seam, requiring reporting on military intercepts over North America in the FY2026 NDAA cycle. Scoring high on this domain means an entity collected, held or adjudicated release of sensor data.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 383 of 1082, score 34). 5 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C1/A3/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** In Citizens Against UFO Secrecy v. NSA the D.C. Circuit sustained NSA's withholding of UFO-related records; the supporting Yeates affidavit was itself released only in redacted form. Source: CAUS v. NSA (CourtListener); NSA collection via The Black Vault
- **Tier 1 documented.** Jon Kosloski, a senior NSA researcher, was appointed to lead AARO in August 2024. Source: DefenseScoop, 26 August 2024
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The FY2026 NDAA cycle included congressional demands for information on military UAP intercepts around North America. Source: DefenseScoop, 10 December 2025

## Defense Appropriations Acts FY2008 and FY2010, the $22M AAWSAP appropriation

Defense Appropriations Acts FY2008 and FY2010, the $22M AAWSAP appropriation is a legislation on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2007 to 2012; axes C0 A3 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: Classified annex appropriation directed by Senate leadership.

### What it is

The funding instrument, and the reason the whole thing stayed invisible. AARO's own history states that 'at the direction of Senate Majority Leader Harry Reid (D-NV), the Defense Appropriations Acts of Fiscal Years 2008 and 2010 appropriated $22 million for the DIA to assess long-term and over-the-horizon foreign advanced aerospace threats.' The money was placed in the classified annex with the agreement of Appropriations chairman Daniel Inouye and senior Republican Ted Stevens, which meant it never appeared in a public budget justification, was never authorised by the Armed Services Committees, and was never subject to floor debate. The structural consequence runs through the entire modern disclosure fight: a program can be created, staffed, contracted and terminated over ten years using appropriated funds without the authorizing committees ever seeing it, and the only reason this one surfaced is that a newspaper published a story in December 2017 and DIA then had to write the Senate Armed Services Committee a letter explaining what it had been doing. The specific bill language and account line have never been released.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 268 of 1082, score 39). 5 connections, 5 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A3/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** 'At the direction of Senate Majority Leader Harry Reid (D-NV), the Defense Appropriations Acts of Fiscal Years 2008 and 2010 appropriated $22 million for the DIA to assess long-term and over-the-horizon foreign advanced aerospace threats.' Source: AARO Historical Record Report Volume 1, 8 March 2024, p.22
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The funds were routed through the classified black budget with Inouye's agreement, bypassing Senate floor debate about the allocation. Source: Honolulu Civil Beat, 13 June 2021
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** Because the appropriation was never matched by an authorization, the Armed Services Committees had no jurisdictional hook to review the program, which is why DIA had to introduce it to them by letter in January 2018, six years after termination. Source: Inference from the appropriations mechanism and the January 2018 DIA letter

- Defense Appropriations Acts FY2008 and FY2010, the $22M AAWSAP appropriation runs out to **Defense Intelligence Agency** (institution, board tier A), a funding link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: $22M appropriated to DIA. The appropriation named DIA as the recipient agency to assess long-term and over-the-horizon foreign advanced aerospace threats. ## United States Department of Homeland Security

United States Department of Homeland Security is a institution on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2002-11-25; axes C2 A3 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: Cabinet department created 2002; the department that stood up and then killed KONA BLUE.

### What it is

DHS was established in 2002, 'combining 22 different federal departments and agencies into a unified, integrated Cabinet agency' with more than 260,000 employees, and its Office of Intelligence and Analysis is a full element of the Intelligence Community. For most of its existence DHS had no visible role in the UAP question. That changed with the declassification of KONA BLUE. According to the joint AARO/DHS paper 'History and Origin of KONA BLUE', KONA BLUE was 'a Prospective Special Access Program (PSAP) that had been proposed to DHS leadership but was never approved or formally established'; in 2011 the DHS Under Secretary for Science and Technology established KONA BLUE as a PSAP, and approximately six months later the DHS Deputy Secretary disapproved and terminated it. AARO's Historical Record Report Volume I states that after DIA cancelled AAWSAP/AATIP in 2012 'its supporters proposed to DHS that they create and fund a new version' to 'restart UAP investigations, paranormal research (including alleged human consciousness anomalies) and reverse-engineer any recovered off-world spacecraft', that interviewees claimed KONA BLUE 'was a sensitive DHS compartment to cover up the retrieval and exploitation of non-human biologics', and that 'Senators Lieberman and Reid asked that the PSAP be established with the promise of additional funding'. AARO's reading is that KONA BLUE proves the recovery narrative was a proposal that got rejected; the opposite reading, which the same document supports, is that a compartment to hold recovered non-human material was requested at Under Secretary level, formally stood up as a prospective program, and killed only when it reached the Deputy Secretary. DHS is also, per AARO, one of the three special access program control office enterprises - DoD, IC and DHS - with which AARO says it partnered to review named programs.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 126 of 1082, score 54). 6 connections, 2 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 2 official denials recorded against it. Axes C2/A3/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** AARO states it partnered with 'the Special Access Program Control Offices for the DoD, IC, and DHS' to review programs identified in interviews by name or description. Source: AARO Historical Record Report Volume I, p. 34
- **Tier 1 documented.** AARO states that interviewees claimed KONA BLUE 'was a sensitive DHS compartment to cover up the retrieval and exploitation of non-human biologics', and that after DIA cancelled AAWSAP/AATIP in 2012 'its supporters proposed to DHS that they create and fund a new version'. Source: AARO Historical Record Report Volume I, pp. 34-35
- **Tier 1 documented.** DHS was 'established in 2002, combining 22 different federal departments and agencies into a unified, integrated Cabinet agency' and employs 'more than 260,000 employees'. Source: DHS, About DHS, retrieved 12 August 2026
- **Tier 1 documented.** In 2011 the DHS Under Secretary for Science and Technology established KONA BLUE as a Prospective Special Access Program; approximately six months later the DHS Deputy Secretary disapproved and terminated it. KONA BLUE was 'never approved or formally established'. Source: AARO/DHS, 'History and Origin of KONA BLUE'
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** The KONA BLUE record establishes that a US department was formally asked, at Under Secretary level and with the support of two sitting senators, to create a compartment for the exploitation of recovered non-human material. Whether the material existed is a separate question from whether the request was made; the request is documented. Reasoning: the AARO/DHS paper is itself the government's admission of the request. Source: Inference from AARO/DHS, 'History and Origin of KONA BLUE'

### Its strongest connections

- United States Department of Homeland Security comes in from **All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office (AARO)** (institution, board tier A), a investigative link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: AARO's SAP review extended to the DHS control office enterprise. AARO names DHS as the third SAP control office enterprise with which it partnered - the only public confirmation that DHS operates a SAP control office at all. Sources: AARO Historical Record Report Volume I (DOPSR-2024-0263), https://media.defense.gov/2024/Mar/08/2003409233/-1/-1/0/DOPSR-2024-0263-AARO-HISTORICAL-RECORD-REPORT-VOLUME-1-2024.PDF, dated 2024-03-08.
- United States Department of Homeland Security runs out to **KONA BLUE** (program, board tier A), a denial link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: DHS Deputy Secretary disapproved and terminated KONA BLUE approximately six months later. The AARO/DHS paper states the Deputy Secretary disapproved and terminated the PSAP roughly six months after it was established, and that KONA BLUE was 'never approved or formally established'. Neither official is named in the released text. Sources: AARO/DHS, 'History and Origin of KONA BLUE', https://www.aaro.mil/Portals/136/PDFs/UAP_RECORDS_RESEARCH/History_and_Origin_of_KONA_BLUE_FINAL_508.pdf, dated 2024-06-01.
- United States Department of Homeland Security comes in from **KONA BLUE** (program, board tier A), a programmatic link at Tier 1 documented. ## High-Frequency Gravitational Wave Communications

High-Frequency Gravitational Wave Communications is a document on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates no dates recorded; axes C0 A0 D0; 5 connections.

In one line: defense-intelligence-reference-document.

### What it is

DIA Defense Intelligence Reference Document (Acquisition Threat Support series), 2010-04-06, DIA-08-1004-005, produced under the Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications (AAWSA/AAWSAP) Program contract with BAASS. Released under FOIA.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 217 of 1082, score 42). 5 connections, 2 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C0/A0/D0.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Author name is withheld in the DIA-released copy under FOIA exemption (b)(6); the "Prepared by:" line is withheld under (b)(3) 10 USC 424. The released cover therefore does not attribute the DIRD to a named author. Source: https://www.dia.mil/FOIA/FOIA-Electronic-Reading-Room/FileId/237646/
- **Tier 1 documented.** Baker published an open-source counterpart in 2010, 'Utilization of High-Frequency Gravitational Waves for Aerospace Systems and Technology', presented at the Seventh Annual AIAA Southern California Aerospace Systems and Technology Conference on 1 May 2010, claiming HFGW applications in communications, surveillance, remote initiation of nuclear events and propulsion. Source: http://www.drrobertbaker.com/docs/Aerospace%20HFGW%20Applications.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** Defense Intelligence Reference Document titled "High-Frequency Gravitational Wave Communications", series "Acquisition Threat Support", published 2010-04-06 with an Intelligence Cut-Off Date (ICOD) of 2009-12-01, DIA document number DIA-08-1004-005. Released by DIA under FOIA and posted in the DIA FOIA Electronic Reading Room. Source: https://www.dia.mil/FOIA/FOIA-Electronic-Reading-Room/FileId/237646/
- **Tier 1 documented.** In 2008 the JASON advisory group reviewed separate HFGW work Baker had done for the Office of the Director of National Intelligence and rejected it: 'The proposed applications of the science of HFGW are fundamentally wrong,' and 'No foreign threat in HFGW is credible.' DIA nevertheless published Baker's HFGW paper as an official Defense Intelligence Reference Document on 6 April 2010. Source: https://www.twz.com/26056/heres-the-list-of-studies-the-militarys-secretive-ufo-program-funded-some-were-junk
- **Tier 1 documented.** Inside cover reads: "This product is one in a series of advanced technology reports produced in FY 2009 under the Defense Intelligence Agency [redacted b(3) 10 USC 424] Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications (AAWSA) Program" and directs questions to the "AAWSA Program Manager, Defense Intelligence Agency, ATTN: [redacted], Bldg 6000, Washington, DC 20340-5100". Source: https://www.dia.mil/FOIA/FOIA-Electronic-Reading-Room/FileId/237646/
- **Tier 1 documented.** Released PDF is 57 pages, marked UNCLASSIFIED//FOR OFFICIAL USE ONLY (FOUO caveat partially illegible in the scan). Source: https://www.dia.mil/FOIA/FOIA-Electronic-Reading-Room/FileId/237646/
- **Tier 1 documented.** The DIA letter of 9 January 2018 lists entry 9 as 'High Frequency Gravitational Wave Communications, Dr Robert Baker, GravWave.' Source: https://irp.fas.org/dia/aatip-list.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The JASON review is hosted by the Federation of American Scientists at irp.fas.org/agency/dod/jason/gravwaves.pdf. Source: https://irp.fas.org/agency/dod/jason/gravwaves.pdf

A further 1 claims sit on this node on the board and are not reproduced here.

### Its strongest connections

- High-Frequency Gravitational Wave Communications comes in from **Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications Program (AAWSAP)** (program, board tier A), a funding link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: DIA money spent on HFGW after JASON's rejection. Tier 2, and the reason is worth stating. A full-text scan of this released DIRD on 23 August 2026 found NO occurrence of the programme name anywhere in the document - unlike 27 of the 32 scanned, whose inside cover names the Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications Program explicitly. The link therefore rests on the DIA's 9 January 2018 list to the Senate Armed Services Committee, which enumerates it among the 38 reference products, and not on the document's own text. The link reads: DIRD. Membership of the DIRD series is established by the released document itself: its cover page carries the Defense Intelligence Reference Document mark and the DIA Defense Futures banner. Document number DIA-08-1004-005. Publication date 6 April 2010 on the cover. 58 pages in the released copy. The cover of the scanned release is degraded, so a document number or date is asserted here only where it reads unambiguously. Sources: Released DIRD, 'High-Frequency Gravitational Wave Communications' (FOIA copy, cover page read 22 August 2026), https://documents2.theblackvault.com/documents/dia/AAWSAP-DIRDs/DIRD_21-DIRD_High-Frequency_Gravitational_Wave_Communications.pdf, dated 2026-08-22; DIA FOIA Electronic Reading Room, AAWSAP/AATIP reference-document entry, https://www.dia.mil/FOIA/FOIA-Electronic-Reading-Room/FileId/237646/, dated 2026-08-22.
- High-Frequency Gravitational Wave Communications runs out to **Signals & Sensor Intelligence** (domain, board tier B), a research link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: subject domain: gravitational-wave communications and surveillance. Baker's open-source 2010 counterpart claims theoretical aerospace, military and civilian applications in 'communications, surveillance, remote initiation of nuclear events and propulsion, including moving space objects and missiles in flight and frustrating anti-missile and anti-satellite systems.' The breadth of that claim is precisely what JASON's 'no foreign threat in HFGW is credible' was rejecting. Sources: Released DIRD, 'High-Frequency Gravitational Wave Communications', FOIA copy, cover page read 22 August 2026, https://documents2.theblackvault.com/documents/dia/AAWSAP-DIRDs/DIRD_21-DIRD_High-Frequency_Gravitational_Wave_Communications.pdf, dated 2026-08-22; DIA FOIA Electronic Reading Room entry for this reference document, https://www.dia.mil/FOIA/FOIA-Electronic-Reading-Room/FileId/237646/, dated 2026-08-22; http://www.drrobertbaker.com/docs/Aerospace%20HFGW%20Applications.pdf, http://www.drrobertbaker.com/docs/Aerospace%20HFGW%20Applications.pdf; https://www.twz.com/26056/heres-the-list-of-studies-the-militarys-secretive-ufo-program-funded-some-were-junk, https://www.twz.com/26056/heres-the-list-of-studies-the-militarys-secretive-ufo-program-funded-some-were-junk
- High-Frequency Gravitational Wave Communications runs out to **JASON** (institution, board tier B), a denial link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: JASON found the underlying physics wrong two years before DIA published the DIRD. In 2008 JASON reviewed separate HFGW work Baker had done for the Office of the Director of National Intelligence and rejected it at the root: 'The proposed applications of the science of HFGW are fundamentally wrong,' and 'No foreign threat in HFGW is credible.' DIA published Baker's HFGW paper as an official Defense Intelligence Reference Document on 6 April 2010 regardless. This is the sharpest available evidence of AAWSAP's quality-control failure: the programme placed on a JWICS-hosted DIA reference series a topic the government's own senior scientific review body had already assessed as wrong and whose threat premise it had declared not credible. It is also the only case in the 38 where an external government review directly contradicts a published deliverable. Sources: Released DIRD, 'High-Frequency Gravitational Wave Communications', FOIA copy, cover page read 22 August 2026, https://documents2.theblackvault.com/documents/dia/AAWSAP-DIRDs/DIRD_21-DIRD_High-Frequency_Gravitational_Wave_Communications.pdf, dated 2026-08-22; DIA FOIA Electronic Reading Room entry for this reference document, https://www.dia.mil/FOIA/FOIA-Electronic-Reading-Room/FileId/237646/, dated 2026-08-22; https://www.twz.com/26056/heres-the-list-of-studies-the-militarys-secretive-ufo-program-funded-some-were-junk, https://www.twz.com/26056/heres-the-list-of-studies-the-militarys-secretive-ufo-program-funded-some-were-junk; https://irp.fas.org/agency/dod/jason/gravwaves.pdf, https://irp.fas.org/agency/dod/jason/gravwaves.pdf
- High-Frequency Gravitational Wave Communications runs out to **Robert M. L. Baker, Jr.** (person, board tier B), a research link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: authored by Robert M. L. Baker Jr., GravWave LLC. The DIA list of 9 January 2018 records entry 9 as 'High Frequency Gravitational Wave Communications, Dr Robert Baker, GravWave' - the earlier KLAS transcription rendered the title as 'Gravity Wave Communication'. The released PDF's author block is redacted under (b)(6). Baker confirmed to researcher Keith Basterfield that he published three related HFGW papers in 2010. ## The Role of Superconductors in Gravity Research

The Role of Superconductors in Gravity Research is a document on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates no dates recorded; axes C0 A0 D0; 5 connections.

In one line: defense-intelligence-reference-document.

### What it is

DIA Defense Intelligence Reference Document (Acquisition Threat Support series), 2010-03-23, DIA-08-1003-013, produced under the Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications (AAWSA/AAWSAP) Program contract with BAASS. Released under FOIA.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 482 of 1082, score 30). 5 connections, 2 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A0/D0.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Author name is withheld in the DIA-released copy under FOIA exemption (b)(6); the "Prepared by:" line is withheld under (b)(3) 10 USC 424. The released cover therefore does not attribute the DIRD to a named author. Source: https://www.dia.mil/FOIA/FOIA-Electronic-Reading-Room/FileId/237631/
- **Tier 1 documented.** Defense Intelligence Reference Document titled "The Role of Superconductors in Gravity Research", series "Acquisition Threat Support", published 2010-03-23 with an Intelligence Cut-Off Date (ICOD) of 2010-12-01, DIA document number DIA-08-1003-013. Released by DIA under FOIA and posted in the DIA FOIA Electronic Reading Room. Source: https://www.dia.mil/FOIA/FOIA-Electronic-Reading-Room/FileId/237631/
- **Tier 1 documented.** Inside cover reads: "This product is one in a series of advanced technology reports produced in FY 2009 under the Defense Intelligence Agency [redacted b(3) 10 USC 424] Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications (AAWSA) Program" and directs questions to the "AAWSA Program Manager, Defense Intelligence Agency, ATTN: [redacted], Bldg 6000, Washington, DC 20340-5100". Source: https://www.dia.mil/FOIA/FOIA-Electronic-Reading-Room/FileId/237631/
- **Tier 1 documented.** The author line on the released cover page is redacted under (b)(6); the inside cover carries the standard AAWSA FY2009 administrative note. Source: https://documents2.theblackvault.com/documents/dia/AAWSAP-DIRDs/DIRD_14-DIRD_The_Role_of_Superconductors_in_Gravity_Research.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The released cover page reads "23 March 2010 / ICOD: 1 December 2010 / DIA-08-1003-013," series "Acquisition Threat Support." The printed Intelligence Cut-Off Date is later than the printed publication date, which is anomalous; every other DIRD in this batch from the same March 2010 tranche carries ICOD 1 December 2009. The reading is recorded as printed and flagged. Source: https://documents2.theblackvault.com/documents/dia/AAWSAP-DIRDs/DIRD_14-DIRD_The_Role_of_Superconductors_in_Gravity_Research.pdf
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Author of record per the DIA letter of 9 January 2018, item 10: "Role of Superconductors in Gravity Research, Dr George Hathaway, Hathaway Consulting." This attribution corrects the common assumption that the paper was written by one of the academic gravity-modification claimants whose work it reviews. Source: https://ufos-scientificresearch.blogspot.com/2019/01/back-to-those-38-defense-intelligence.html, DEMOTED from Tier 1 (cited to a secondary/encyclopedic source); needs primary replacement
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The paper reviews the rotating-superconductor gravity-modification claims associated with Evgeny Podkletnov and the work of Ning Li. Hathaway had personally run a Podkletnov replication between 1996 and 2003 and obtained null results, so DIA commissioned the review from a researcher with a documented record of failing to reproduce the effect. Source: https://www.altpropulsion.com/gravity-control-experiments/

Its inside cover reads 'This product is one in a series of advanced technology reports produced in FY 2009 under the Defense Intelligence Agency ... Advanced Aerospace Weapon System Applications (AAWSA) Program' - the redaction between them is (b)(3):10 USC 424. Verified by downloading this DIRD and scanning its full text on 23 August 2026. The scans are OCR-degraded, so the quotation is normalised from the damaged rendering. The link reads: DIRD. Membership of the DIRD series is established by the released document itself: its cover page carries the Defense Intelligence Reference Document mark and the DIA Defense Futures banner. Publication date 23 March 2010 on the cover. 17 pages in the released copy. The cover of the scanned release is degraded, so a document number or date is asserted here only where it reads unambiguously. - The Role of Superconductors in Gravity Research runs out to **Materials and custody analysis** (domain, board tier A), a research link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: subject domain: superconducting materials and claimed gravitational coupling. The paper's subject is a materials question posed as a physics question: whether a rotating high-temperature superconductor can produce a measurable gravitational anomaly. It sits with the materials strand of the DIRD set rather than with the metric-engineering strand. Sources: Released DIRD, 'The Role of Superconductors in Gravity Research', FOIA copy, cover page read 22 August 2026, https://documents2.theblackvault.com/documents/dia/AAWSAP-DIRDs/DIRD_14-DIRD_The_Role_of_Superconductors_in_Gravity_Research.pdf, dated 2026-08-22; DIA FOIA Electronic Reading Room entry for this reference document, https://www.dia.mil/FOIA/FOIA-Electronic-Reading-Room/FileId/237631/, dated 2026-08-22.
- The Role of Superconductors in Gravity Research comes in from **George D. Hathaway** (person, board tier C), a research link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: author of record on the DIA list. DIA list item 10: "Role of Superconductors in Gravity Research, Dr George Hathaway, Hathaway Consulting." This is the attribution most likely to be got wrong from the title alone: the paper reviews the Podkletnov and Ning Li gravity-modification claims but was not written by either of them or by any academic advocate of the effect. It was written by the Toronto engineer who had run the Podkletnov replication and reported null results. ## The Disclosure Project

The Disclosure Project is a program on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 1993; axes C0 A0 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: Private witness-aggregation and congressional-hearing campaign, 1993-.

### What it is

Founded by Steven Greer in 1993 as an offshoot of his Center for the Study of Extraterrestrial Intelligence, the Disclosure Project's method was to locate former military, intelligence, contractor and air traffic personnel with first-hand UFO experience, record their testimony on video, and use the aggregate to demand congressional hearings with subpoena power and witness amnesty. It briefed members of Congress and officials in Washington in April 1997 - the same week the Wilson-Davis notes later record as the trigger for Admiral Wilson's inquiries - and staged its major public event at the National Press Club on 9 May 2001 with roughly twenty witnesses and a stated corpus of more than four hundred. No hearing followed. The project's structural contribution is the demonstration that witness aggregation without documents does not move institutions: the 2001 event produced global coverage and zero congressional action, whereas the 2023 Grusch testimony - one witness with an Inspector General complaint, a security clearance and a statutory whistleblower channel - produced a hearing. The lesson the modern movement learned from Greer is that the paperwork is the lever.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 883 of 1082, score 17). 5 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A0/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** The Clinton White House's principal UFO FOIA collection (2006-0474-F) consists in the majority of documents sent to the White House by Steven M. Greer of CSETI. Source: Clinton Digital Library collection finding aid 2006-0474-F
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** On 9 May 2001 the Disclosure Project presented roughly twenty former military and government witnesses at the National Press Club and called for congressional hearings with subpoena power and amnesty for witnesses. Source: Disclosure Project National Press Club event, 9 May 2001, and contemporaneous international press coverage
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** The Disclosure Project's April 1997 Washington briefing week is the proximate trigger recorded in the Wilson-Davis notes for Admiral Thomas Wilson's 1997 attempt to locate the special access programme. Source: Inference from the sequence recorded in the Wilson-Davis notes, which place Wilson's inquiry immediately after exposure to the Greer briefing material.

### Its strongest connections

- The Disclosure Project comes in from **Mark McCandlish** (person, board tier A), a witness-chain link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: Disclosure Project witness, testimony taken December 2000. His December 2000 testimony is printed in the project's own April 2001 Briefing Document at section 3.8.5, under the heading 'Testimony of Mr. Mark McCandlish, US Air Force / December 2000'. Sources: Disclosure Project Briefing Document, section 3.8.5, https://archive.org/stream/DisclosureProjectBriefingDocument/DisclosureProjectBriefingDocument_djvu.txt, dated 2001-04.
- The Disclosure Project comes in from **Disclosure Project Briefing Document, April 2001** (document, board tier C), a institutional link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: The project's own publication. It is the Disclosure Project's own April 2001 compilation of witness testimony. Sources: Disclosure Project Briefing Document, section 3.8.5, https://archive.org/stream/DisclosureProjectBriefingDocument/DisclosureProjectBriefingDocument_djvu.txt, dated 2001-04.
- The Disclosure Project comes in from **Steven M. Greer** (person, board tier B), a institutional link at Tier 2 named person on the record. The link reads: Greer founded and led the Disclosure Project. Greer founded CSETI in 1990 and the Disclosure Project in 1993, culminating in the 9 May 2001 National Press Club event. ## 341st Strategic Missile Wing histories, 1967-68 (FOIA release, 14 June 2001)

341st Strategic Missile Wing histories, 1967-68 (FOIA release, 14 June 2001) is a document on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2001-06-14; axes C1 A2 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: Excerpts from five quarterly 341st Strategic Missile Wing histories, released to John Greenewald Jr by HQ 341st Space Wing on 14 June 2001.

### What it is

Excerpts from five quarterly histories of the 341st Strategic Missile Wing, Malmstrom AFB, released to John Greenewald Jr by Headquarters 341st Space Wing on 14 June 2001 and posted by The Black Vault. The January to March 1967 volume carries a section headed 'the investigation of Echo Flight incident and the results' at pages 32 to 40, footnoted throughout to a single classified engineering report of 23 March 1967. The release is the document the board's Malmstrom node names as decisive. It states the shutdown happened and states that the UFO reports around it were disproven, and it names no launch officer on duty.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 306 of 1082, score 37). 5 connections, 2 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A2/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** No launch control officer is named anywhere in the eighty-seven page release: the only officer named in the Echo Flight section is 'Capt. Bradshaw in the command post', and the names Figel, Salas and Carlson do not appear. Source: 341st Space Wing FOIA release of 14 June 2001, full text, https://documents.theblackvault.com/documents/ufos/malmstromufo.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The history records that the 801st Radar Squadron, Malmstrom AFB, 'gave a negative report on any radar or atmospheric interference problems related to Echo Flight', and that a comparable multi-facility shutdown had occurred at Alpha Flight on 19 December 1966 with the same capsule crew, adverse weather and a commercial power failure. Source: 341st Strategic Missile Wing History, 1 Jan - 31 Mar 1967, pp 38 and 40, https://documents.theblackvault.com/documents/ufos/malmstromufo.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented, allegation shaped wording as stored.** The same history states at page 38: 'Rumors of Unidentified Flying Objects (UFO) around the area of Echo Flight during the time of fault were disproven', and records that a Mobile Strike Team questioned about the morning of 16 March 1967 'stated that no unusual activity or sightings were observed'. Source: 341st Strategic Missile Wing History, 1 Jan - 31 Mar 1967, p 38, https://documents.theblackvault.com/documents/ufos/malmstromufo.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The wing history records that on 16 March 1967 at 0845 all sites in Echo Flight shut down with No-Go indication of Channels 9 and 12 on the Voice Reporting Signal Assemble, that all launch facilities in E Flight 'lost strategic alert nearly simultaneously', and that no other Wing I configuration lost alert at that time. Source: 341st Strategic Missile Wing History, 1 Jan - 31 Mar 1967, p 32, in the 341st Space Wing FOIA release of 14 June 2001, https://documents.theblackvault.com/documents/ufos/malmstromufo.pdf

## Relatório de Ocorrências, Comando Aéreo de Defesa Aérea, 2 June 1986 (Arquivo Nacional BR_DFANBSB_ARX_0_0_0249)

Relatório de Ocorrências, Comando Aéreo de Defesa Aérea, 2 June 1986 (Arquivo Nacional BR_DFANBSB_ARX_0_0_0249) is a document on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1986-06-02 to 2009-09-25; axes C1 A2 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: CONFIDENCIAL Brazilian Air Defence Command report; declassified 25 September 2009.

### What it is

An eight-page document on Ministério da Aeronáutica letterhead, stamped CONFIDENCIAL on every page, addressed by the Commander of the Air Defence Air Command to the Commander General of the Air, dated Brasília-DF, 2 June 1986, signed by Brigadeiro do Ar José Pessoa Cavalcanti de Albuquerque as acting commander COMDA/NuCOMDABRA, and annexed with a Relatório de Ocorrências. It is scanned and served publicly by the Brazilian National Archives under reference BR_DFANBSB_ARX_0_0_0249 and was declassified on 25 September 2009, twenty-three years after the minister of aeronautics promised a full dossier within thirty days. Its section IV, Considerações Finais, is the operative text: it lists six behavioural characteristics observed and then states the Command's own opinion that the phenomena are solid and reflect a form of intelligence. It also praises the response posture, noting that each engaged air unit maintained an alert aircraft at 45 minutes' readiness and that within under thirty minutes of activation armed vectors were available. It is a document in which a national air defence command tells its own chain of command, in writing and under classification, that it has tracked solid, intelligently behaving unidentified objects that outran supersonic interceptors.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 292 of 1082, score 38). 5 connections, 3 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A2/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Considerações Finais para 3: the Command's opinion that the phenomena 'são sólidos e refletem de certa forma inteligência ... não forçosamente tripulados'. Source: Arquivo Nacional BR_DFANBSB_ARX_0_0_0249
- **Tier 1 documented.** Document header: 'MINISTÉRIO DA AERONÁUTICA, COMANDO AÉREO DE DEFESA AÉREA ... Brasília-DF, 02 de Junho de 1986. Do Comandante. Ao Exmo Sr Comandante do Comando Geral do Ar. Assunto: Relatório de Ocorrência.' Classification marking [CONFIDENCIAL] on each page. Source: Arquivo Nacional BR_DFANBSB_ARX_0_0_0249, page images
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The report was released on 25 September 2009. Source: BBC Brasil, 14 January 2022; Wikipedia '1986 Brazilian UFO incident'

### Its strongest connections

- Relatório de Ocorrências, Comando Aéreo de Defesa Aérea, 2 June 1986 (Arquivo Nacional BR_DFANBSB_ARX_0_0_0249) runs out to **Noite Oficial dos OVNIs - the Brazilian 'Night of the UFOs', 19 May 1986** (event, board tier A), a programmatic link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: the official record of the night. Its Considerações Finais record solid radar-and-visual objects that outran supersonic interceptors and 'reflect in a certain manner intelligence'. Sources: Arquivo Nacional PDF, http://imagem.sian.an.gov.br/acervo/derivadas/BR_DFANBSB_ARX/0/0/0249/BR_DFANBSB_ARX_0_0_0249_d0001de0001.pdf, dated 1986-06-02.
- Relatório de Ocorrências, Comando Aéreo de Defesa Aérea, 2 June 1986 (Arquivo Nacional BR_DFANBSB_ARX_0_0_0249) comes in from **Brigadeiro do Ar José Pessoa Cavalcanti de Albuquerque** (person, board tier B), a custody link at Tier 1 documented. ## DoD special access programme governance change, 5 January 1994

DoD special access programme governance change, 5 January 1994 is a event on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1994-01-05; axes C2 A3 D1; 5 connections.

In one line: SAPOC, Senior Review Group and SAPCO stood up under the Deputy Secretary of Defense.

### What it is

On 5 January 1994 the Deputy Secretary of Defense changed the Department's special access programme management and control structures, standardising the approval, termination, revalidation and restructuring of SAPs through a Special Access Program Oversight Committee chaired by the Deputy Secretary, with a Senior Review Group established to support the SAPOC principals and a Special Access Program Coordinating Office staffing both. By November 1994 the committee was chaired by Deputy Secretary John Deutch with the Under Secretary for Acquisition and Technology, the Under Secretary for Policy, the Vice Chairman of the Joint Chiefs of Staff and the Assistant Secretary for C3I as permanent, non-delegable members. The Joint Security Commission, reporting to the Secretary of Defense and the Director of Central Intelligence on 28 February 1994, described the committee as recently formed and supported it. This node exists because the board's sapoc node dates the committee to a 2010 directive, and the 2010 issuance re-established rather than created it.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 223 of 1082, score 42). 5 connections, 1 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A3/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** A central Army special-access oversight office predates the 1994 reform by ten years. GAO/NSIAD-88-152 records: 'A DOD review of Army special access programs in 1983 revealed systemic weaknesses in various management areas, which resulted in establishment of a central oversight office in September 1984.' The same report states that in 1988 'The Deputy Under Secretary of Defense for Policy is required to maintain a listing of all special access programs, but does not do so because of security concerns', and that 'the Secretary's Office has no centralized office with cognizance of all special access programs'. The January 1994 dates this board carries stand; the Army aftermath began in 1983-84. Source: GAO/NSIAD-88-152, B-230522, 24 May 1988, Results in Brief and Recommendations, https://www.gao.gov/assets/nsiad-88-152.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** A commission chartered by the Secretary of Defense and the Director of Central Intelligence corroborated the formation within two months: 'Until recently there has been no procedure for centralized assessment of special program proposals submitted directly to the Deputy Secretary of Defense by the military departments. The recent formation of the DoD Special Access Program Oversight Committee, which the Commission fully supports, will ensure that every program is reviewed by a panel of senior officials prior to its establishment, and annually thereafter, to determine whether compartmentation for each program is still required.' Source: Joint Security Commission, 'Redefining Security: A Report to the Secretary of Defense and the Director of Central Intelligence', 28 February 1994, chapter 2, https://sgp.fas.org/library/jsc/chap2.html
- **Tier 1 documented.** The same chapter records the scale and the carve-outs the board's oversight view argues from: 'Some of the most sensitive DoD programs are "waived" or "carved out" from certain oversight and administrative requirements. There are over one hundred DoD SAPs, with many having numerous compartments and subcompartments', and each SAP manager 'is free to establish the security rules that will apply to his or her particular program.' On congressional reporting the Commission concluded it saw 'no need to modify existing reporting procedures'. Source: Joint Security Commission, 'Redefining Security', 28 February 1994, chapter 2, https://sgp.fas.org/library/jsc/chap2.html
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The annual review and the decision authority date from this change, not from the 2010 directive: 'All SAPs will be reviewed and validated annually for continued SAP status by the OSD SAPOC. ... The DEPSECDEF is the final decision maker in establishing, disestablishing or restructuring SAPs.' Source: Deputy Secretary of Defense, SAPOC Information Bulletin, November 1994, https://sgp.fas.org/othergov/sapoc.html
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The change and its date: 'On January 5th, 1994, the Deputy Secretary of Defense (DEPSECDEF) significantly enhanced changes in the DoD Special Access Program (SAP) management and control structures. ... The plan standardized and formalized the SAP approval, termination, revalidation and restructuring process through the Special Access Program Oversight Committee (SAPOC). A Senior Review Group (SRG) was established to support the SAPOC principals.' Tier 2 because the text opened is a transcription published by the Federation of American Scientists and no scan of the original bulletin was found from this environment. Source: Deputy Secretary of Defense, Special Access Program Oversight Committee, Information Bulletin, November 1994, as transcribed at https://sgp.fas.org/othergov/sapoc.html
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The committee's membership in November 1994: 'The SAPOC is chaired by the Honorable John Deutch, DEPSECDEF, with permanent members the Honorable Paul Kaminski USD(A&T), the Honorable Walter Slocombe USD(P), ADM William A. Owens, VCJCS, and the Honorable Emmett Paige Jr., ASD(C3I). BGEN Mike Kostelnik, OUSD(A&T), serves as both member and executive secretary to the SAPOC in his capacity as director of the SAPCO (SAP Coordinating Office). SAPOC membership is not further delegable.' Source: Deputy Secretary of Defense, SAPOC Information Bulletin, November 1994, https://sgp.fas.org/othergov/sapoc.html

## DoDD 5205.07, Special Access Program (SAP) Policy (1 Jul 2010, Chg 2 of 4 Feb 2020)

DoDD 5205.07, Special Access Program (SAP) Policy (1 Jul 2010, Chg 2 of 4 Feb 2020) is a document on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2010-07-01 to 2024-09-12; axes C1 A3 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: dod-issuance.

### What it is

This is the version of DoD's SAP charter that governed for the entire period covered by the Elizondo, Grusch and Kirkpatrick record, and it is the version cross-referenced by the NISPOM rule at 32 CFR 117.6(b)(3). It reissued the 5 January 2006 directive, established the Special Access Program Oversight Committee, established the DoD SAP Central Office under the authority, direction and control of the Deputy Secretary of Defense, and set out in plain language the three things this board most needs documented. First, the taxonomy: its glossary defines an acknowledged SAP as one 'whose existence is acknowledged but its specific details (technologies, materials, techniques, etc.) are classified'; an unacknowledged SAP as one 'having protective controls ensuring the existence of the program is not acknowledged, affirmed, or made known to any person not authorized for such information'; and a waived SAP as 'A SAP for which the Secretary of Defense has waived applicable reporting in accordance with Reference (c) following a determination of adverse effect to national security. An unacknowledged SAP that has more restrictive reporting and access controls.' Reference (c) is section 119 of title 10. Second, the congressional access rule: members of the defense and intelligence committees are authorised access to DoD SAPs 'except for waived programs', and for waived SAPs 'only the chair, the ranking minority member, and the staff directors' are authorised access unless the Secretary or Deputy Secretary approves otherwise. Third, the custody rule: 'All waived SAP material submitted to Congress, to include annual reports and other products, shall remain in DoD custody. No copies shall be stored outside DoD repositories or control without the approval of the Secretary or Deputy Secretary of Defense.' A committee briefed on a waived program is not permitted to keep the paper.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 58 of 1082, score 74). 6 connections, 2 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A3/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** CONGRESS KEEPS NOTHING. Para 4.h verbatim: 'All waived SAP material submitted to Congress, to include annual reports and other products, shall remain in DoD custody. No copies shall be stored outside DoD repositories or control without the approval of the Secretary or Deputy Secretary of Defense.' A briefed chairman therefore retains no document, no notes in committee custody, and no institutional memory once he leaves the chair. ## DoDD 5205.07, DoD Special Access Program (SAP) Policy (12 Sep 2024)

DoDD 5205.07, DoD Special Access Program (SAP) Policy (12 Sep 2024) is a document on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2024-09-12; axes C1 A3 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: dod-issuance.

### What it is

The current directive, effective 12 September 2024, cancels the 1 July 2010 version as amended and incorporates the Deputy Secretary of Defense memorandum 'Implementation Guidance for DoD Special Access Program Enterprise Reform' of 11 July 2023. Two changes matter for this board. First, the governance structure is retained and strengthened: the SAPOC remains 'the senior governing body of the DoD SAP Enterprise for governance, management, and oversight of DoD SAPs', chaired by the Deputy Secretary of Defense, with the Director of DoD SAPCO as Executive Secretary of the SAPOC and the SRG and Chair of the SSWG, reporting directly to the Deputy Secretary. Establishment of new SAP umbrellas and compartments is stated to be 'by exception' and requires Secretary or Deputy Secretary approval. Second, and much more significant, the 2024 directive replaces the acknowledged / unacknowledged / waived taxonomy with two new categories - 'Joint Force Integration SAP', defined as 'A SAP intended for greater integration and sharing across the Joint Force and the broader SAP community', and 'Strategic Enabler SAP', defined as 'A SAP protecting capabilities or information that enable strategic competition and warrant more limited and restrictive sharing'. The words acknowledged, unacknowledged, waived and carve-out do not appear in its definitions section. This does not repeal 10 U.S.C. 119(e), which continues to authorise the reporting waiver, and it does not repeal 32 CFR 117.6(b)(3), which continues to authorise the carve-out and still cites this very directive by number. The effect is that the two mechanisms most relevant to a concealed program survive in statute and in the Code of Federal Regulations while ceasing to be defined in the Department's own public charter - the terminology has been removed from the shop window without being removed from the shop. The directive also carries forward the congressional-contact reporting rule: 'All personnel accessed to DoD SAPs will notify the Director, DoD SAPCO ... when briefing or providing DoD SAP material to any member of Congress.'

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 79 of 1082, score 68). 6 connections, 1 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 2 official denials recorded against it. Axes C1/A3/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** CONTRACTOR SPEECH RESTRICTION. The directive states that no DoD contractor entity 'will provide SAP material to any members of Congress or their staff without Director, DoD SAPCO approval.' The Director, DoD SAPCO is designated the primary point of contact for congressional SAP matters and 'Maintains records of DoD SAP information provided to Congress.' Source: https://www.esd.whs.mil/Portals/54/Documents/DD/issuances/dodd/520507p.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** DoDD 5205.07 (12 September 2024) cancels DoD Directive 5205.07 of 1 July 2010, as amended, and incorporates the Deputy Secretary of Defense Memorandum, 'Implementation Guidance for DoD Special Access Program Enterprise Reform', 11 July 2023. Source: DoDD 5205.07, 12 September 2024, cover page
- **Tier 1 documented.** DoDD 5205.07 (2024) defines 'Joint Force Integration SAP' as 'A SAP intended for greater integration and sharing across the Joint Force and the broader SAP community' and 'Strategic Enabler SAP' as 'A SAP protecting capabilities or information that enable strategic competition and warrant more limited and restrictive sharing.' Source: DoDD 5205.07, 12 September 2024, Glossary G.2
- **Tier 1 documented.** DoDD 5205.07 (2024) describes the SAPOC as 'the senior governing body of the DoD SAP Enterprise for governance, management, and oversight of DoD SAPs', chaired by the Deputy Secretary of Defense. Source: DoDD 5205.07, 12 September 2024, section 3.2.a
- **Tier 1 documented.** DoDD 5205.07 (2024) makes the Director, DoD SAPCO the 'primary point of contact for SAP matters with ... the Legislative Branch' and requires that 'All personnel accessed to DoD SAPs will notify the Director, DoD SAPCO ... when briefing or providing DoD SAP material to any member of Congress.' Source: DoDD 5205.07, 12 September 2024, Director DoD SAPCO responsibilities
- **Tier 1 documented.** DoDD 5205.07 (2024) section 3.1.d: 'Establishment of new SAP umbrellas and compartments will be by exception. Establishment ... requires Secretary of Defense or Deputy Secretary of Defense approval.' Source: DoDD 5205.07, 12 September 2024, section 3.1.d
- **Tier 1 documented.** Header block: DoD Directive 5205.07, 'Special Access Program Policy'; effective September 12, 2024; releasability 'Cleared for public release'; 'Reissues and Cancels: DoD Directive 5205.07, July 1, 2010, as amended'; approved by Kathleen H. Hicks, Deputy Secretary of Defense. Source: https://www.esd.whs.mil/Portals/54/Documents/DD/issuances/dodd/520507p.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** NEGATIVE FINDING WITH CONSEQUENCE. A full-text pass of the 12 September 2024 directive found no occurrence of the terms 'carve-out', 'waived', 'unacknowledged', 'notification', or '10 U.S.C. 119'. The 2024 reissuance moved every operative concealment mechanism out of the public-release Directive and into the companion Instruction, DoDI 5205.11, which remains public but is the document one must know to ask for. Source: https://www.esd.whs.mil/Portals/54/Documents/DD/issuances/dodd/520507p.pdf
