## ICD 906, Controlled Access Programs (ODNI)

ICD 906, Controlled Access Programs (ODNI) is a document on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2015-10-17; axes C2 A3 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: ic-directive.

### What it is

ICD 906 is the directive that governs what the intelligence community calls its special access programs. Its authority is 'The National Security Act of 1947, as amended; Executive Order (EO) 12333, as amended; and other applicable provisions of law', and it 'establishes the oversight and management of Intelligence Community (IC) special access programs pursuant to EO 13526, Section 4.3, or any successor document, referred to within the IC as Controlled Access Programs (CAPs)'. The reissued version obtained for this board supersedes the ICD 906 signed 17 October 2015; the released PDF was generated on 19 December 2024 and its signature block is an image that does not resolve to text, so the exact reissue date is not machine-readable in the public copy. The substance is unambiguous and, for this board, extraordinary. Only the DNI or the Principal Deputy DNI may establish, validate, modify or terminate a CAP. CAPs are defined as 'a top-level control system (such as Special Intelligence, Talent Keyhole, Human Intelligence Control System), and any compartment or sub-compartment under a control system'. Unacknowledged CAPs exist and 'may be established only where knowledge of the existence of a program would reveal aspects of its protected information ... Unacknowledged CAPs may be established only with the approval of the DNI or PDDNI'. Governance mirrors DoD: a CAP Oversight Committee chaired by the PDDNI or designee, and a Senior Review Group. All CAPs are to be recorded in Scattered Castles, the IC personnel security database - 'Any exemption from this requirement shall be approved only by the DNI or PDDNI'. Access may be limited by billet or 'by name or position (BYNOP)'. Critically, ICD 906 assigns the ODNI Controlled Access Program Central Office responsibility for 'the coordination of CAP-related engagements, to include, but not limited to, congressional briefings, requests from the Information Security Oversight Office, and the National Security Council' - and nowhere in the directive is there any counterpart to 10 U.S.C. 119: no annual report deadline, no 30-day pre-initiation notice, no statutory list. The IC side of the architecture has less congressional reporting machinery than the DoD side, not more. ICD 906 also expressly does not apply to SAPs established by the Secretaries of State, Defense, Energy or Homeland Security or the Attorney General, 'Nor does this Directive apply to special access programs under the purview of the National Security Council, such as covert action' - identifying, in one sentence, a third category of program governed by neither regime.

### The board's reading

Why the intelligence side is the harder problem and the softer target at the same time. Harder: ICD 906 has no statutory annual report deadline, no 30-day pre-initiation clock and no publicly reported program list, and unacknowledged CAPs may be created on the signature of two people, the DNI or the Principal Deputy DNI. Softer: since 15 March 2022 the statute at 50 U.S.C. 3091a requires semiannual briefings on every controlled access program in effect to the intelligence and appropriations committees and congressional leadership, requires annual reports listing every active and every terminated compartment and sub-compartment with a DNI certification that each is substantiated and justified and a named control officer for each, contains no waiver authority whatsoever, and since 23 December 2024 cuts off National Intelligence Program funding for any CAP established without notification. On paper the IC has the strongest special-access oversight regime in the United States government and the DoD has the weakest. ICD 906 also does two things of direct forensic value: it requires that all CAPs and all individual accesses be recorded in Scattered Castles, the IC personnel security database, with exemptions approvable only by the DNI or PDDNI and reviewed annually - so a person's access history is a queryable record - and it expressly disclaims application to programs 'under the purview of the National Security Council, such as covert action', identifying the one remaining space that neither SAP regime reaches.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 48 of 1082, score 81). 7 connections, 2 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A3/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** ANNUAL RE-JUSTIFICATION OF UNACKNOWLEDGED STATUS. Policy D.10.e.(1): 'For unacknowledged CAPs, IC elements shall include in their annual review submission, a justification for why each such CAP still meets the requirements to be unacknowledged.' D.10.b: 'Any new CAPs not submitted, and approved, will be terminated.' D.10.e: 'CAPs not re-validated shall be terminated and the protected information shall be de-compartmented or protected by a validated CAP.' Source: https://web.archive.org/web/20250112183207/https://www.dni.gov/files/documents/ICD/ICD-906-Controlled-Access-Programs.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** IC UNACKNOWLEDGED PROGRAMS. Policy D.3, verbatim: 'unacknowledged CAPs may be established only where knowledge of the existence of a program would reveal aspects of its protected information, and when there is no other means to protect information about programs at the collateral classification level, or when required by statute. Unacknowledged CAPs may be established only with the approval of the DNI or PDDNI.' Source: https://web.archive.org/web/20250112183207/https://www.dni.gov/files/documents/ICD/ICD-906-Controlled-Access-Programs.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** ICD 906 contains no annual congressional reporting deadline, no pre-initiation notification period and no statutory reporting clock equivalent to 10 U.S.C. 119. Source: Full text of ICD 906, by omission
- **Tier 1 documented.** ICD 906 section B.1: this directive 'establishes the oversight and management of Intelligence Community (IC) special access programs pursuant to EO 13526, Section 4.3, or any successor document, referred to within the IC as Controlled Access Programs (CAPs).' Source: ICD 906, section B.1
- **Tier 1 documented.** ICD 906 section B.2: 'This ICD supersedes ICD 906, Controlled Access Programs, signed 17 October 2015.' Source: ICD 906, section B.2
- **Tier 1 documented.** ICD 906 section C.3: the directive does not apply to SAPs established by the Secretaries of State, Defense, Energy or Homeland Security or the Attorney General, and 'Nor does this Directive apply to special access programs under the purview of the National Security Council, such as covert action.' Source: ICD 906, section C.3
- **Tier 1 documented.** ICD 906 section D.2 defines CAPs as 'a top-level control system (such as Special Intelligence, Talent Keyhole, Human Intelligence Control System), and any compartment or sub-compartment under a control system within the IC.' Source: ICD 906, section D.2

## Kirkpatrick, 'Here's What I Learned as the U.S. Government's UFO Hunter' (Scientific American)

Kirkpatrick, 'Here's What I Learned as the U.S. Government's UFO Hunter' (Scientific American) is a document on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2024; axes C0 A1 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: Op-ed, 19 January 2024, seven weeks after leaving AARO.

### What it is

Published 19 January 2024, seven weeks after Kirkpatrick left AARO and seven weeks before the office published the Historical Record Report Volume I he had authored. It is the clearest single statement of the containment thesis in the former director's own voice. Its arguments: the investigation found no evidence of aliens, only allegations circulated repeatedly by UFO claim advocates; the public whistleblowers never provided evidence to AARO despite invitations and are therefore 'suspect' for preferring to be 'sensationalist in the public eye'; the narrative is 'a textbook example of circular reporting, with each person relaying what they heard, but the information often ultimately being sourced to the same small group of individuals'; the claims derive from 'a small group of interconnected believers' influenced by Robert Bigelow and originating in the defunct AATIP; and belief in a statement is proportional to the volume and repetition of its transmission rather than to the facts. The document matters for three reasons. It is a former officeholder attacking named living witnesses in a scientific journal within weeks of departure. It previews the conclusions of an official report not yet published, which is a rollout technique rather than an analysis. And its central mechanism - circular reporting from a small group - is precisely the mechanism the Department would confirm eighteen months later in the Yankee Blue disclosure, except that the small group seeding the belief was the United States Air Force.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 380 of 1082, score 34). 6 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 3 official denials recorded against it. Axes C0/A1/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 2 named person on the record, allegation shaped wording as stored.** Argues the allegations derive from inadvertent disclosures of legitimate US programs or from unsupported belief, and describes the milieu as circular reporting among interconnected believers. Source: Sean Kirkpatrick, Scientific American opinion, 19 January 2024
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** In a follow-on interview, suggested Grusch may have misinterpreted information or fallen under the influence of believers, and called the situation a self-licking ice cream cone. Source: Kirkpatrick interview with Peter Bergen, 23 January 2024
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Kirkpatrick wrote that the crash-retrieval narrative is 'a textbook example of circular reporting' sourced to the same small group of individuals and traceable to Robert Bigelow and AATIP. Source: Sean Kirkpatrick, Scientific American, 19 January 2024
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Kirkpatrick wrote that whistleblowers who prefer to be 'sensationalist in the public eye' rather than bring evidence to AARO are 'suspect'. Source: Sean Kirkpatrick, Scientific American, 19 January 2024
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** The op-ed's publication seven weeks before Volume I, by an author who had already left the office, previews the report's conclusions in a venue with scientific prestige and no right of reply - a rollout technique, not an analysis. Source: Inference from the publication sequence: departure 1 December 2023, op-ed 19 January 2024, report 8 March 2024

## KLAS-TV (Las Vegas)

KLAS-TV (Las Vegas) is a institution on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 1981; axes C0 A0 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: CBS affiliate, Channel 8.

### What it is

KLAS-TV is the Las Vegas station where George Knapp has worked as anchor and I-Team investigative reporter since 1981, apart from a break from 1991 to 1995. It is where the Bob Lazar interviews aired in May and November 1989, putting Area 51 into global consciousness, and it remains the institutional base from which Knapp has run four decades of UAP reporting. A local CBS affiliate in a desert market being the originating venue for the most durable claims in this field is itself a structural fact about where this story has been allowed to live.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 951 of 1082, score 14). 5 connections, 1 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A0/D2.

- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Knapp joined KLAS-TV as reporter and news anchor in 1981, left in 1991 and was rehired in 1995; the Bob Lazar programmes aired on KLAS in May and November 1989. Source: Wikipedia, 'George Knapp (television journalist)', retrieved 12 August 2026, DEMOTED from Tier 1 (cited to a secondary/encyclopedic source); needs primary replacement

The link reads: Knapp testified as KLAS-TV chief investigative reporter. Knapp opened his sworn statement: 'I am George Knapp, chief investigative reporter at KLAS-TV in Las Vegas. I began my pursuit of this weird mystery way back in 1987, and for 38 years, I have always approached this as a news story. It is not a matter of faith or belief to me.' ## Brasilia, Federal District, Brazil

Brasilia, Federal District, Brazil is a location on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates no dates recorded; axes C1 A1 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: Federal capital of Brazil; seat of the Ministry of Defence and the Arquivo Nacional holdings.

### What it is

Brasilia is the seat of the Brazilian federal government and the administrative centre from which the Brazilian Air Force's unidentified aerial phenomena files were released into the Arquivo Nacional, making Brazil one of the few states to have placed military UFO records in a citizen-accessible public archive. The 1986 incident of 19 May, in which Brazilian Air Force fighters were scrambled against multiple radar-tracked objects and the Air Minister subsequently briefed the press, is the country's principal documented case and its records trace back through this capital. Brasilia functions on this board as the control case for what state transparency on this subject actually looks like when it is attempted.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 717 of 1082, score 23). 5 connections, 1 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A1/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Brazilian military UFO records have been transferred into the Arquivo Nacional and are publicly accessible. Source: Arquivo Nacional information system
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** Referenced by the board but no dossier written yet. This node exists so the graph resolves and so the gap is visible rather than silent, see the wave-3 queue. Source: Board bookkeeping

### Its strongest connections

- Brasilia, Federal District, Brazil runs out to **Arquivo Nacional (Brazil) - UFO/UAP collection** (institution, board tier A), a custody link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: The UFO fund is held in Brasília and its reference code says so. The Arquivo Nacional's own notice identifies the holding as 'o Fundo Objeto Voador Não Identificado – OVNI (BR DFANBSB ARX)', produced by the Comando da Aeronáutica and held by the Arquivo Nacional, comprising 743 registros. DFANBSB is the archival code for the Arquivo Nacional's Brasília unit. RQS 193/2022 states the transfer route directly: 'A partir de 2007, a Aeronáutica, mas não o Exército e nem a Marinha, passou a enviar seus papeis resultantes de suas pesquisas para o Arquivo Nacional, em Brasília.' ## Eglin Air Force Base, Florida

Eglin Air Force Base, Florida is a location on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1935; axes C2 A2 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: USAF installation; site of the 2023 protected disclosure.

### What it is

The Air Force installation in Florida's first congressional district that produced the only UAP incident a member of Congress has personally investigated on site. Matt Gaetz's office received a protected disclosure from Eglin; he travelled there with Burchett and Luna and was initially refused images, radar and full flight-crew access before obtaining a photograph and an interview with one crew member. The incident involved a test mission over the Gulf of Mexico in airspace that was supposed to be clear, and a formation of four craft in a diamond plus an orb. Gaetz described the radar sequence as one he alone in Congress had seen and asked that the material be subpoenaed. Luna returned to Eglin in her September 2025 opening statement, describing 'the infamous Eglin Air Force Base incident' and stating that multiple active-duty Air Force pilot whistleblowers alleged that the Air Force was covering up UAP activity there. As of August 2026 the Eglin radar and imagery have not been produced to the committee.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 426 of 1082, score 32). 5 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A2/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Gaetz recommended that the Eglin radar images and crew photograph be provided to the Committee, 'subpoenaed if necessary'; no such production has been recorded. Source: CHRG-118hhrg53022, 26 July 2023
- **Tier 1 documented.** Gaetz: the flight crew 'were out on a test mission that day over the Gulf of Mexico and when you are on a test mission you are supposed to have clear airspace... And they saw a sequence of four craft in a clear diamond formation for which there is a radar sequence that I and I alone have observed in the U.S. Congress.' Source: GPO CHRG-118hhrg53022, 26 July 2023
- **Tier 1 documented, allegation shaped wording as stored.** Luna: 'the infamous Eglin Air Force Base incident that occurred when myself and former Representative Matt Gates, as well as Representative Burchett, followed up on a lead from multiple active duty Air Force pilot whistleblowers that alleged that the United States Air Force was covering up UAP activity at Eglin Air Force Base.' Source: GPO CHRG-119hhrg61718, 9 September 2025

## Nevada Test Site / Nevada National Security Site

Nevada Test Site / Nevada National Security Site is a location on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 1951; axes C2 A3 D0; 5 connections.

In one line: DOE site complex; successive contractor management EG&G, Bechtel Nevada, NSTec.

### What it is

The Nevada complex is where the classification regimes overlap. A Department of Energy site operated under Atomic Energy Act authorities by a succession of private management and operating contractors - EG&G in the nuclear testing era, Bechtel Nevada from 1996 to 2006, the Northrop Grumman-led National Security Technologies LLC from 2006 - it sits adjacent to the Air Force operating location near Groom Lake, whose environmental disclosure obligations the President waives annually. Its contractor-operated laboratories, the Remote Sensing Laboratory and the Special Technologies Laboratory, hold the national capability for characterising anomalous radiological and material signatures. Al O'Donnell, the EG&G manager who told George Knapp that a recovered saucer and a live being existed, was the first general manager of EG&G's operations here. Material held under DOE Restricted Data authority at a contractor-operated site sits outside the 10 U.S.C. Sec. 119 special access program reporting regime entirely.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 594 of 1082, score 27). 5 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A3/D0.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Bechtel Nevada and subsequently National Security Technologies, LLC appear as the management and operating research organisations of record on DOE Nevada Test Site technical reports. Source: OSTI.GOV, Nevada Test Site Environmental Report 2005
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Al O'Donnell, described as the first general manager of EG&G in Nevada 'which managed the Nevada test site,' is the source of the recovered-saucer and live-being account. Source: George Knapp, sworn testimony 9 September 2025 and Liberation Times, 28 February 2023

### Its strongest connections

- Nevada Test Site / Nevada National Security Site comes in from **United States Department of Energy** (institution, board tier A), a institutional link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: DOE operating authority over the Nevada National Security Site. The Nevada test complex is DOE/NNSA property operated by contract, with its own protective force and clearance regime, and is named repeatedly in retrieval testimony. Sources: NNSA - About NNSA, https://www.energy.gov/nnsa/about-nnsa, dated 2026-08-12.
- Nevada Test Site / Nevada National Security Site comes in from **Atomic Energy Commission** (institution, board tier A), a geographic link at Tier 1 documented. The link reads: Area 51 was added to the Nevada Test Site by the Commission. The same CIA history states that Eisenhower approved the addition of the strip known by its map designation as Area 51 to the Nevada Test Site, and DNA 6040F separately locates Groom Lake at the northeast corner of the AEC's Nevada Proving Ground. ## Manned Orbiting Laboratory and the DORIAN / KH-10 reconnaissance payload

Manned Orbiting Laboratory and the DORIAN / KH-10 reconnaissance payload is a program on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1963-12 to 1969-06-10; axes C1 A3 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: USAF programme with an NRO black mission; announced December 1963, cancelled 10 June 1969, declassified October 2015.

### What it is

A fully documented case of a real programme carrying a second, classified programme inside it, closed by the government and then released by the agency that ran it. Publicly MOL was an Air Force laboratory to determine the military usefulness of men in space; inside it sat DORIAN, an NRO reconnaissance system handled in the BYEMAN control system and designated KH-10 in 1969. Seventeen officers were astronaut-trained between 1965 and 1969, on a modified Gemini-B with a hatch through the heat shield, for polar launches from Vandenberg on the Titan-IIIM. None of them flew. The programme was cancelled on 10 June 1969 with 13,187 cleared contractor personnel on it, and the NRO declassified almost the entire DORIAN collection in October 2015. It is the strongest available answer to a question this board is repeatedly asked, which is what a secret space programme actually looks like on the record. Two opened sources disagree on one number and the disagreement is recorded rather than resolved: the NRO volume gives a 70-inch aperture and NASA gives a 72-inch diameter primary mirror.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 212 of 1082, score 43). 5 connections, 2 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A3/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** NASA's history office dates the programme's public and classified halves: announced by Defense Secretary Robert S. McNamara in December 1963, formally approved by President Johnson in August 1965, 17 pilots selected in three groups on 12 November 1965, 17 June 1966 and 30 June 1967, flown on the Titan-IIIM from Vandenberg into polar orbit, cancelled 10 June 1969, and 'not declassified by the NRO until October 2015'. Source: John Uri, '55 Years Ago: Manned Orbiting Laboratory Cancellation', NASA, 11 June 2024, https://www.nasa.gov/history/55-years-ago-manned-orbiting-laboratory-cancellation/
- **Tier 1 documented.** Seventeen officers were trained and none flew: 'Between 1965 and 1969, quietly and without fanfare, 17 non-NASA individuals were astronaut-trained in order to meet the reconnaissance needs of the United States.' The camera is dated and designated: 'In 1969, the Dorian camera system was designated KH-10 within the Talent-Keyhole community.' Source: Spies in Space, NRO CSNR, May 2019, introduction and chapter 3, https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/GOVPUB-D-PURL-gpo123652/pdf/GOVPUB-D-PURL-gpo123652.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The cover and the mission are described by the agency itself. The NRO's volume says MOL was 'open "white" world, but maintained a highly-classified "black" mission', with the reconnaissance mission 'codenamed Dorian in the BYEMAN security system', and its foreword calls Dorian 'a deeply classified program managed by the then darkly hidden agency of the Intelligence Community, the National Reconnaissance Office'. Source: Spies in Space: Reflections on National Reconnaissance and the Manned Orbiting Laboratory, NRO Center for the Study of National Reconnaissance, May 2019, foreword and chapter 3, https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/GOVPUB-D-PURL-gpo123652/pdf/GOVPUB-D-PURL-gpo123652.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The declassification is dated and quantified in the NRO's own words: on 22 October 2015 the CSNR held a panel at the National Museum of the United States Air Force 'on the occasion of the NRO declassifying almost its entire collection of records on the Dorian program. There were over 850 documents with some 22,000 pages and 280 photographs.' Source: Spies in Space, NRO CSNR, May 2019, foreword, https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/GOVPUB-D-PURL-gpo123652/pdf/GOVPUB-D-PURL-gpo123652.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** The scale of the clearance at termination: 'On 10 June 1969, 13,187 contractor personnel were cleared on MOL and faced reassignment.' Source: Spies in Space, NRO CSNR, May 2019, chapter 4, https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/GOVPUB-D-PURL-gpo123652/pdf/GOVPUB-D-PURL-gpo123652.pdf

## MIT Lincoln Laboratory

MIT Lincoln Laboratory is a institution on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 1951; axes C2 A3 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: FFRDC, est. 1951; Air Force sponsored.

### What it is

Lincoln Laboratory is MITRE's parent and the first FFRDC in this domain to have publicly agreed to hand a UFO record to Congress. It was established in 1951 to build SAGE, the continental air defence system, and MITRE was spun out of its Division 6 in 1958 to carry that work forward. It is administered by MIT, sponsored by the Under Secretary of Defense for Research and Engineering since a sponsor change on 25 April 2011, and was funded at roughly 1.36 billion dollars in FY2024. In 1952 Lincoln Laboratory hosted the Beacon Hill Study, the classified summer study group on aerial reconnaissance that shaped American overhead collection for a generation. On 8 May 2026 Rep. Eric Burlison wrote to Lincoln Laboratory asking it to locate, preserve and arrange archival review of a reel-to-reel recording identified as 'AF-ATIC-FILM, 03/52', labelled 'flying saucer talk', in which Project Blue Book director Edward J. Ruppelt briefed the Beacon Hill group - months before the July 1952 Washington, DC radar incidents. By 25 June 2026 Burlison announced that Lincoln Laboratory had agreed to disclose it. That is the first documented case of an FFRDC producing a UAP record to Congress because Congress named the file by its exact designation. Burlison's own account of the method - 'We told MIT Lincoln Labs we know the exact video file you are holding, by name, and we want it' - is the operational lesson of this entire domain: FFRDC holdings are reachable only when the requester already knows what to ask for.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 159 of 1082, score 49). 5 connections, 1 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A3/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Founded in 1951 to build the SAGE air defense network; one of the oldest FFRDCs, a class of contractor with access to classified and proprietary information that for-profit contractors are legally barred from seeing. Source: The Hill/NewsNation, 16 May 2026
- **Tier 1 documented.** Lincoln Laboratory is an MIT-administered FFRDC sponsored by DoD USD(R&E), with the sponsor change recorded on 25 April 2011, funded at approximately $1,360.6 million in FY2024. Source: NSF NCSES Master Government List of FFRDCs; CRS Report R44629 funding table
- **Tier 1 documented.** MITRE was spun out of Lincoln Laboratory in 1958 to manage SAGE deployment, taking 485 Lincoln Laboratory employees with it. Source: MITRE's SAGE history site; MIT Lincoln Laboratory SAGE history page
- **Tier 1 documented.** On 8 May 2026 Rep. Burlison wrote to MIT Lincoln Laboratory requesting preservation and review of a reel-to-reel recording identified as 'AF-ATIC-FILM, 03/52' and labelled 'flying saucer talk', featuring Edward J. Ruppelt as briefer, and asked the laboratory to work with the National Archives on preservation and archival review. Source: Burlison press release, 8 May 2026
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Burlison's MITRE press release names the Beacon Hill Study alongside 'AF-ATIC-FILM, 03/52', MIT Lincoln Laboratory, NARA and Record Group 615. Source: Burlison press release, 26 May 2026
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** By 25 June 2026 Lincoln Laboratory had agreed to make the material available; the Disclosure Foundation describes it as a reel-to-reel audio recording relating to the July 1952 Washington sightings and says Lincoln Lab's lawyers confirm it exists. Source: The Hill/NewsNation, 29 June 2026
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Lincoln Laboratory's attorneys agreed to comply within 30 days with Rep. Burlison's May 2026 demand for a classified 1952 briefing recording referenced as a 'flying saucer talk.' Source: Rep. Burlison on X, 15 May 2026, quoted by The Hill/NewsNation, 16 May 2026
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** On 25 June 2026 Burlison announced that MIT Lincoln Laboratory had agreed to disclose the 1952 recording, quoting his own approach: 'We told MIT Lincoln Labs we know the exact video file you are holding, by name, and we want it.' Source: Rep. Eric Burlison via X, reported by Cybernews, 25 June 2026

### Its strongest connections

## National Archives UAP Records Collection (Record Group 615)

National Archives UAP Records Collection (Record Group 615) is a program on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2023; axes C1 A2 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: Created under FY2024 NDAA sections 1841-1843, P.L. 118-31.

### What it is

The statutory records collection, and the quietest failure in this domain. Sections 1841 through 1843 of the FY2024 National Defense Authorization Act, Public Law 118-31, directed the establishment of an Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena Records Collection at the National Archives. NARA created Record Group 615 to receive it and issued guidance to federal agencies on identifying, preparing and transferring UAP records into that record group. What NARA does not publish is the thing that would make the collection meaningful: which agencies have actually transferred anything, how much, or when. Its public position, verbatim, is that 'NARA will continue to add UAP records to the UAP Records Collection (RG 615) and make them available online through the National Archives Catalog on an ongoing, rolling basis as they are received from federal agencies.' There is no published compliance list, no page or item counts by agency, no transfer dates, and no statement identifying any agency as delinquent. The Department of War asserts progress - AARO 'has made progress in making UAP information available and in transferring those records to the National Archives in accordance with federal law' - but the assertion is unverifiable against NARA's own pages. The Burlison FY2027 NDAA amendment exists precisely to fix this: statutory transfer timelines, a Senate-confirmed review board with subpoena power, and contractor reach. A records collection with no compliance reporting is a collection whose emptiness is indistinguishable from its completeness.

### The board's reading

Status as of 12 August 2026. Statutory basis: FY2024 NDAA, P.L. 118-31, sections 1841-1843. Record Group: RG 615, Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena Records Collection. NARA has issued guidance to federal agencies on identification, preparation and transfer of UAP records into RG 615, and publishes FAQs and bulk catalog downloads specific to UAP records. NARA's published position is that it will add records 'on an ongoing, rolling basis as they are received from federal agencies.' What is not published, and what this board records as the central gap in the statutory channel: no agency compliance list, no per-agency item or page counts, no transfer dates, and no identification of any agency as delinquent. AARO's UAP Records page directs the public to the NARA collection, and the Department of War asserts AARO 'has made progress in making UAP information available and in transferring those records to the National Archives in accordance with federal law' - an assertion that cannot be checked against anything NARA publishes.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 300 of 1082, score 37). 5 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C1/A2/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** 'NARA will continue to add UAP records to the UAP Records Collection (RG 615) and make them available online through the National Archives Catalog on an ongoing, rolling basis as they are received from federal agencies.' Source: National Archives UAP records page
- **Tier 1 documented.** NARA publishes no list of which agencies have transferred records, no item or page counts by agency, and no transfer dates. Source: National Archives UAP Records Collection and UAP research topic pages, accessed 12 August 2026
- **Tier 1 documented.** The collection was established under sections 1841-1843 of the FY2024 National Defense Authorization Act, Public Law 118-31, and NARA created Record Group 615 to hold it. Source: National Archives UAP Records Collection page, accessed 12 August 2026
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** The Department of War states that AARO 'has made progress in making UAP information available and in transferring those records to the National Archives in accordance with federal law.' Source: Department of War news story, 27 February 2026
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** Without published agency-by-agency transfer accounting, no one outside government can distinguish a collection that agencies are populating from one they are ignoring - which is the specific defect the Burlison FY2027 amendment's statutory timelines and subpoena-empowered review board are designed to remove. Source: Inference from NARA's rolling-basis statement against the disclosure architecture in the adopted FY2027 NDAA amendment

## FLIR1, Gimbal and GoFast Navy gun-camera videos

FLIR1, Gimbal and GoFast Navy gun-camera videos is a document on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 2004 to 2020; axes C2 A2 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: Three ATFLIR clips: one from November 2004, two from January 2015.

### What it is

Three unclassified U.S. Navy infrared targeting-pod recordings, one captured in November 2004 during the USS Nimitz encounters and two in January 2015 associated with the USS Theodore Roosevelt operating area. Elizondo cleared the three files internally on a DD Form 1910 on 9 August 2017, approved 24 August 2017. Christopher Mellon has said on the record that he received the videos from a Defense Department official in the Pentagon parking lot and gave them to the New York Times. Two appeared with the Times story of 16 December 2017 and the third, GoFast, was published in March 2018; TTSA hosted them. On 10 September 2019 the Navy's Joseph Gradisher stated that 'the Navy designates the objects contained in these videos as unidentified aerial phenomena' while also saying the Navy had never released them publicly. On 27 April 2020 the Department of Defense authorised their release outright, stating that 'the aerial phenomena observed in the videos remain characterized as unidentified.' That is the full arc: cleared internally, leaked, published by a private company, designated UAP by the Navy, and finally released by the Department itself.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 218 of 1082, score 42). 5 connections, 3 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A2/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Navy spokesman Joseph Gradisher stated on 10 September 2019: 'The Navy designates the objects contained in these videos as unidentified aerial phenomena.' Source: U.S. Navy statement, 10 September 2019
- **Tier 1 documented.** On 27 April 2020 DoD authorised the release of three unclassified Navy videos, one taken in November 2004 and two in January 2015, stating 'the aerial phenomena observed in the videos remain characterized as unidentified.' Source: Department of Defense statement, 27 April 2020
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Christopher Mellon stated he received the videos from a Defense Department official in the Pentagon parking lot and provided them to the New York Times. Source: Christopher Mellon in The Phenomenon (2020), reported by Vice, 20 October 2020

### Its strongest connections

The link reads: Cleared the GoFast, Gimbal and FLIR files for internal distribution only. The form's stated purpose expressly excludes publication: research and analysis only, for a UAS threat database. This is the narrow basis on which the Navy later said no unrestricted release was ever authorised. Sources: Blue Blurry Lines, https://www.blueblurrylines.com/2019/08/documents-released-via-foia-on-aatip.html, dated 2019-08-17.

## P.L. 118-31 Subtitle C, Secs. 1841-1843 (what survived)

P.L. 118-31 Subtitle C, Secs. 1841-1843 (what survived) is a legislation on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2023; axes C1 A2 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: Enacted UAP records regime, 22 December 2023.

### What it is

What Congress actually enacted is a records-transfer statute with the enforcement removed. Sec. 1841 orders the Archivist to establish the Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena Records Collection, requires physical integrity and original provenance to be preserved, requires public availability within 30 days of transmission and online within 180 days thereafter, and assigns continuing oversight to six committees. Sec. 1842 bars destruction, alteration or mutilation of any UAP record, bars withholding or reclassification of anything already public, bars withholding of records created outside the Federal Government, and gives agency heads 300 days to review and organise. Sec. 1843 is where it fails. Postponement may be ordered where 'the original classification authority makes a determination that there is clear and convincing evidence' of harm, the clear and convincing standard survives, but the person applying it is the classifier rather than an independent board, which converts a check into a self-certification. Senior Agency Officials may unilaterally withdraw records from the Collection on a determination that they are both unrelated to UAP and properly classified, needing only to notify Congress 60 days beforehand. Where disclosure is postponed the agency head must notify congressional leadership within 15 days with a reason. There is no appeal, no de novo review, no subpoena and no mechanism reaching material rather than paper.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 157 of 1082, score 49). 5 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 2 official denials recorded against it. Axes C1/A2/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Sec. 1841(a)(1)(C) puts the phrases 'technologies of unknown origin' and 'non-human intelligence' into enacted federal law as categories of record the Archivist must collect, '(or equivalent subjects by any other name with the specific and sole exclusion of temporarily non-attributed objects)'. Source: PLAW-118publ31 sec. 1841(a)(1)(C), 137 Stat. 699-700
- **Tier 1 documented.** Sec. 1842(a)(2) prohibits destruction, alteration or mutilation of any UAP record and bars reclassification of anything already publicly disclosed before enactment. Source: P.L. 118-31, Sec. 1842(a)(2), GPO
- **Tier 1 documented.** Sec. 1842(g)(2)(D) requires full public disclosure of each UAP record within 25 years of its first creation unless the President personally certifies identifiable harm outweighing the public interest. Source: PLAW-118publ31 sec. 1842(g)(2)(D), 137 Stat. 704-705

## NDAA FY2026 UAP provisions (Secs. 1671-1673)

NDAA FY2026 UAP provisions (Secs. 1671-1673) is a legislation on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2025-12-18; axes C1 A2 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: December 2025: reporting requirements repealed, classification guides to be inventoried.

### What it is

The National Defense Authorization Act for Fiscal Year 2026 was approved on 18 December 2025. Its UAP provisions run in two opposite directions at once and both matter to the money question. Section 1672, 'Elimination of Duplicative Reporting Requirements Relating to Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena', repeals section 413 of the Intelligence Authorization Act for Fiscal Year 2022 (50 U.S.C. 3373a) outright, and rewrites section 1683(f)(1)(A) of the FY2022 NDAA so that the DNI and the Secretary of Defense 'shall jointly require that each element of the intelligence community and component of the Department of Defense with data relating to unidentified anomalous phenomena makes such data available immediately to the Office in a manner that protects intelligence sources and methods.' Section 1673, 'Accounting of Security Classification Guides Relating to Unidentified Anomalous Phenomena', directs the Director of AARO within 180 days of enactment - by roughly 16 June 2026 - to make an accounting of the security classification guides that apply to information used for UAP reports and investigations, and permits issuance of a consolidated security classification matrix. Section 1671 requires briefings on intercepts of UAP by NORAD and USNORTHCOM. What is absent is as important as what is present: neither the FY2025 nor the FY2026 NDAA re-enacted the section 1687(a) bar on funds for unreported UAP special access activity, which by its terms applied only to funds authorised by the FY2024 Act. The permanent piece - the IR&D indirect-cost prohibition at section 1687(b), codified as a 50 U.S.C. 3373 note - survives; the annual funding bar did not.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 69 of 1082, score 70). 6 connections, 1 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A2/D3.

### The claims on the record

Source: https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/PLAW-119publ60/html/PLAW-119publ60.htm
- **Tier 1 documented.** Public Law 119-60 (FY2026 NDAA, approved 18 December 2025), Div. Source: PLAW-119publ60 table of contents and secs. 1671-1673
- **Tier 1 documented.** Sec. 1671(b) requires the first post-enactment briefing to cover NORAD/USNORTHCOM UAP intercepts 'not previously provided that occurred during the period beginning on January 1, 2004'. Source: PLAW-119publ60 sec. 1671(b)

## NORAD and US Northern Command

NORAD and US Northern Command is a institution on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1958; axes C0 A3 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: Homeland air defence combatant command.

### What it is

The command responsible for detecting and defeating air threats to North America, and the institution whose public statements since 2023 constitute the government's most damaging admissions on the observational record. NORAD counted more than 600 drone reports above US military installations since 2022. Its commander since February 2024, Gen. Gregory Guillot, made counter-UAS the centrepiece of a 90-day assessment driven by the Langley incursions, established the Falcon Peak counter-drone demonstration series, and stated on the record that he knew of no 'organized or unorganized foreign nexus' to Langley. In February 2023 NORAD-controlled fighters destroyed three unidentified objects over Alaska, the Yukon and Lake Huron in four days, none of which were recovered intact and two of which an allied government's own Secret memorandum described as unverified as to function and propulsion. By March 2026 Guillot confirmed detections over installations had increased year on year with roughly a quarter defeated. A homeland air defence command that cannot attribute persistent intrusions over its own bomber and fighter bases is the strongest available argument that the observational record is a live national-security problem rather than a historical curiosity.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 513 of 1082, score 29). 5 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C0/A3/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** NORAD continued issuing intercept press releases through 2026, including multiple temporary flight restriction violation intercepts in the Northeast and over New Jersey. Source: NORAD Newsroom press releases, 2026
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Guillot established Falcon Peak, a counter-drone demonstration with multiple vendors, and advocates a layered approach including directed energy. Source: The War Zone, 30 October 2024
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** NORAD recorded more than 600 drone reports above US military installations since 2022. Source: NORAD via The War Zone, 30 October 2024

## Oak Ridge National Laboratory (UT-Battelle, LLC)

Oak Ridge National Laboratory (UT-Battelle, LLC) is a institution on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 1943; axes C3 A2 D1; 5 connections.

In one line: DOE FFRDC administered by UT-Battelle, LLC since 1 April 2000; Oak Ridge, TN; contract DEAC0500OR22725.

### What it is

Oak Ridge is where the modern government sent the material. In 2022 the All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office contracted ORNL to conduct materials testing on a magnesium alloy specimen publicly alleged to have come from a crashed extraterrestrial vehicle in 1947 and to exhibit extraordinary properties. ORNL's findings were published as 'ORNL Synopsis: Analysis of a Metallic Specimen' in April 2024 and released publicly in July 2024: the specimen was a terrestrial magnesium alloy, though neither AARO nor ORNL could verify the specimen's historical origin, and records of failed magnesium alloy designs from the relevant era were described as scant. ORNL is administered by UT-Battelle, LLC - a partnership of the University of Tennessee and Battelle Memorial Institute - under DOE contract DEAC0500OR22725, recorded on USAspending at approximately 42.3 billion dollars across the life of the contract, and was funded at roughly 2.09 billion dollars in FY2024. In December 2023, AARO's founding director Sean Kirkpatrick left government and immediately became Chief Technology Officer for Defense and Intelligence Programs at ORNL - the laboratory that had performed AARO's materials analysis on his watch. That the government's only publicly acknowledged analysis of alleged recovered material was performed by a Battelle-operated laboratory, and that the official who commissioned it then went to work there, is the single most concrete instance on this board of the FFRDC layer absorbing both the evidence and the officials.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 145 of 1082, score 51). 7 connections, 3 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C3/A2/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** AARO states: 'In 2022, The All-domain Anomaly Resolution Office (AARO) contracted with Oak Ridge National Laboratory (ORNL) to conduct materials testing on a magnesium (Mg) alloy specimen.' Source: AARO UAP Records/Information Papers page, aaro.mil
- **Tier 1 documented.** Sean Kirkpatrick departed AARO on 1 December 2023 and became Chief Technology Officer for Defense and Intelligence Programs at Oak Ridge National Laboratory. Source: Public record of Kirkpatrick's departure and appointment
- **Tier 1 documented.** UT-Battelle, LLC has administered Oak Ridge National Laboratory since 1 April 2000 under DOE contract DEAC0500OR22725, with recorded obligations of approximately $42.3 billion and a period of performance running to 2030; ORNL was funded at approximately $2,091.2 million in FY2024. Source: NSF NCSES Master Government List of FFRDCs; USAspending.gov; CRS Report R44629
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Neither AARO nor ORNL could verify the specimen's historical origin, and records of failed magnesium alloy designs are scant. Source: Gizmodo reporting on the published ORNL synopsis, 11 July 2024
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** On American Alchemy with Jesse Michels, 2 April 2025, at 1:09:33, the retired Senate Armed Services Committee staffer Kirk McConnell said that the contract forming AARO's advisory group 'was let through Oak Ridge' while Sean Kirkpatrick directed AARO. ## Project MOON DUST

Project MOON DUST is a program on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 1961; axes C3 A2 D2; 5 connections.

In one line: USAF/State Dept recovery and exploitation program.

### What it is

MOON DUST was the US Air Force codeword for the collection and exploitation of objects and debris of unknown origin that survived re-entry - nominally Soviet and other foreign space hardware, in practice an all-source recovery capability with a standing tasking to acquire material that fell from the sky. It ran from the early 1960s under Air Force Intelligence, with State Department reporting channels supporting the overseas collection, and it is documented in released Department of State cable traffic, one file of which was declassified by CIA. Its significance for this board is jurisdictional rather than evidential: MOON DUST establishes that from at least 1961 the United States maintained a named, funded, worldwide programme whose purpose was to go and get whatever came down, with a chain of custody that ran into Air Force technical intelligence at Wright-Patterson - the same institutional address that the Twining memorandum used in 1947. The Air Force told Congress and researchers for years that MOON DUST had nothing to do with unidentified objects; the programme's own charter language on unidentified fallen material is why that answer never held. MOON DUST and its air-transport twin BLUE FLY are the connective tissue between the Blue Book era and everything that follows: Blue Book was the public-facing sighting file; MOON DUST was the retrieval function, and it did not close in 1969.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 135 of 1082, score 53). 5 connections, 4 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C3/A2/D2.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Department of State records on Project MOON DUST have been released under FOIA, including a 22-page file and a two-page file declassified by the Central Intelligence Agency. Source: The Black Vault, Project Moon Dust document archive, 4 Mar 2015
- **Tier 1 documented.** MOON DUST is officially described as an operation to recover objects and debris from space vehicles that survived re-entry. Source: Department of State releases, Project Moon Dust files
- **Tier 1 documented.** The figures commonly quoted as MOON DUST recoveries are notifications of deorbit received, not objects recovered. The 1127th's own history records that SPADATS 'notified AFNIAA of the deorbit of 37 Soviet space objects' in the first half of 1967, and the companion history of the Assistant Chief of Staff, Intelligence records 'notifications of the deorbit of 49 Soviet space objects (rocket bodies, payloads, and space platforms), and of 17 US space objects considered to be of special importance' in the second half. Deorbit notification is the tasking trigger; recovery is a separate act neither document counts. Source: History, 1127 USAF Field Activities Group, 1 Jan - 30 Jun 1967, p.30; and History of the Assistant Chief of Staff, Intelligence, July - December 1967, p.62
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** MOON DUST's retrieval tasking survived the 1969 closure of Project Blue Book, meaning the United States retained a recovery capability after publicly terminating its investigative one. Source: Inference from the programme's charter scope, the existence of post-1969 State Department MOON DUST cable traffic, and the absence of any published termination order.

## Project SIGN

Project SIGN is a program on the board; board tier B, documented channel and capability, programme role indicated but not yet nailed to a primary source; dates 1947 to 1949; axes C2 A3 D1; 5 connections.

In one line: First official USAF UFO project (1948-1949).

### What it is

Project SIGN was the first official United States Air Force investigation of unidentified flying objects, directed on 30 December 1947 on the recommendation of Lt. Gen. Nathan Twining's memorandum of 23 September 1947 and beginning operations at Wright Field on 23 January 1948. It sat inside Air Materiel Command intelligence under Col. Howard McCoy. SIGN's significance is that its staff reached the interplanetary conclusion and were overruled: in mid-1948 the project produced a Top Secret 'Estimate of the Situation' concluding the objects were of extraterrestrial origin, which Air Force Chief of Staff Gen. Hoyt Vandenberg rejected for insufficient proof and ordered destroyed. The Condon Report, written under Air Force contract twenty years later, records this. SIGN was reconstituted as Project GRUDGE on 11 February 1949 with an explicitly dismissive posture, and the personnel who had signed the Estimate were dispersed. The SIGN episode establishes the pattern that governs the whole legacy era: the working-level analysts closest to the data reach an exotic conclusion, and the conclusion is destroyed rather than rebutted.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded B on a global pass (rank 164 of 1082, score 48). 5 connections, 3 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C2/A3/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** Project SIGN transitioned to Project GRUDGE on 11 February 1949. Source: Condon Report, Section V Chapter 2
- **Tier 1 documented.** Staff prepared a classified 'Estimate of the Situation' in 1948 asserting UFOs were interplanetary vehicles; Gen. Vandenberg rejected it for lacking proof and copies were destroyed. Source: Condon Report, Section V Chapter 2
- **Tier 1 documented.** Twining's letter of 23 September 1947 directed the establishment of a study of UFOs, and the project received priority designation in a December 1947 letter. Source: Condon Report, Section V Chapter 2
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** SIGN operated under Air Materiel Command intelligence at Wright Field, with J. Allen Hynek retained as scientific consultant from 1948. Source: Ruppelt, 'The Report on Unidentified Flying Objects' (1956); Project Blue Book administrative records, NARA RG 341

The link reads: AARO finds SIGN, GRUDGE and Blue Book sightings were ordinary objects. The 2024 report concludes most sightings across all the legacy programs were ordinary objects and misidentification, and that no official review has ever confirmed extraterrestrial technology. Sources: AARO Historical Record Report, Volume I, https://www.aaro.mil/Portals/136/PDFs/AARO_Historical_Record_Report_Vol_1_2024.pdf, dated 2024-02-01.

## PURSUE Release 05

PURSUE Release 05 is a event on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2026; axes C2 A3 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: 7 August 2026; 41 files (16 videos, 22 documents, 3 images); 1950/1953-2026.

### What it is

The fifth and most recent tranche, published 7 August 2026: 41 files comprising 16 videos, 22 documents and 3 images, with a 130MB document bundle and a 513MB video bundle. The portal states the span as 1953 to 2026; the underlying content reaches back to 1950. Its centrepiece is the Gulf of Oman incident of 8 September 2021 - six videos from an AC-130J gunship together with intelligence report DOW-UAP-D101 documenting approximately 25 instances of UAP, described as cold orbs roughly four feet in diameter flying at between 250 and 1,300 mph at low altitude, and reported as reacting to the aircraft's cannon fire. That is a US military gunship engaging unidentified objects and recording their response, released by the Department that engaged them. The tranche also contains DOW-UAP-PR134, a January 2025 video of a small orb over a residential area in the Middle East; FBI-UAP-D031, a black triangular craft with a red light at each corner near a military base at Colorado Springs in October 2023; digital renderings of triangular UAP sightings from 2002, 2011 and 2023; a CIA report on an alleged November 1963 Brazil recovery of a metal sphere containing a deceased occupant in a spacesuit, subsequently deemed unsubstantiated by the American Consulate; and a 1953 US Navy Photographic Interpretation Center analysis of the 1950 Great Falls, Montana and 1952 Tremonton, Utah films. The Navy PIC finding is the most consequential single document in the entire PURSUE corpus: after more than a thousand hours of frame analysis, the Navy's own photo interpreters assessed the Utah objects as bright, silvery reflective disks that were self-luminous and intelligently controlled and inconsistent with natural phenomena or commonly known aerospace technologies. That is an affirmative technical finding by a US Navy analytic unit, released by the Department of War in 2026, and it directly contradicts the disposition the same films received from the Robertson Panel in January 1953.

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 119 of 1082, score 55). 5 connections, 5 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A3/D3.

## Reid Letter to DEPSECDEF Requesting Restricted SAP Status

Reid Letter to DEPSECDEF Requesting Restricted SAP Status is a document on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 2009; axes C2 A3 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: Senator Harry Reid to William J. Lynn III, 24 June 2009.

### What it is

Three pages on Senate letterhead that are, in the board's judgement, the most consequential unclassified document in the funded lineage. Reid, as Senate Majority Leader, asks Deputy Secretary of Defense William Lynn III to establish 'a Restricted Special-Access-Program (SAP) with a Bigoted Access List for specific portions of the AATIP', and then itemises what needs protecting: methodologies for identifying and engineering advanced technologies; personnel allocation and oversight details; application and engineering approaches; and the involvement of private contractors and industry partners. He proposes a preliminary bigoted list for FY10 against a program length projected FY09 through FY13, and refers questions to his staffer Bob Herbert at (202) 437-3162. The letter uses 'Advanced Aerospace Threat and Identification Program', a name that appears nowhere on the contract. Its significance is what it implies about what Reid had been told: a bigoted access list is the most restrictive personnel control in the US classification system, reserved for programs whose very participant roster is itself classified, and Reid was asking for one over 'engineering advanced technologies' and contractor involvement. DoD said no. Susan Gough authenticated the letter on 13 June 2019 while stating it changed nothing.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 158 of 1082, score 49). 5 connections, 1 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 2 official denials recorded against it. Axes C2/A3/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** DoD spokesperson Susan Gough confirmed the letter's authenticity on 13 June 2019. Source: Susan Gough to The Black Vault, 13 June 2019
- **Tier 1 documented.** Reid requested 'a Restricted Special-Access-Program (SAP) with a Bigoted Access List for specific portions of the AATIP', naming protectable areas including methodologies for identifying and engineering advanced technologies and the involvement of private contractors and industry partners. Source: Reid to Lynn, 24 June 2009, DIA FOIA release
- **Tier 1 documented.** The letter projects program length FY09 through FY13 with a preliminary bigoted list for FY10, and refers questions to Reid staffer Bob Herbert. Source: Reid to Lynn, 24 June 2009
- **Tier 3 stated inference.** A bigoted access list is used where the identity of cleared personnel is itself the sensitive fact; requesting one over a program whose deliverables were 38 unclassified literature reviews indicates Reid was protecting something not represented in those deliverables. Source: Inference from classification practice applied to the letter's stated protection categories

## RIAS, Inc. (Research Institute for Advanced Studies)

RIAS, Inc. (Research Institute for Advanced Studies) is a institution on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates no dates recorded; axes C1 A1 D0; 5 connections.

In one line: The Glenn L. Martin Company's industrial research institute, Baltimore; gravitation was explicitly its subject.

### What it is

RIAS is the cleanest documented instance on this board of an aerospace prime setting up a research institute to study gravitation, and the documentation is a participants list and a dated letter rather than an allegation. The 1957 Chapel Hill conference on the role of gravitation in physics prints two of its staff among the attendees -- 'Witten, L. | RIAS, Inc., Baltimore' and 'Bass, R.W. | RIAS, Inc., Baltimore' -- and the institutional style there is corporate: RIAS, Inc., a company in Baltimore, in a room otherwise full of universities.

Its purpose is stated by the executive who proposed it. George S. Trimble wrote to Bryce DeWitt on 10 June 1955 that 'our industry was vitally concerned with gravity' and that 'At least one category of the things one must study, when he desires to bring space flight to reality, is the laws of nature surrounding the force of gravity', describing the proposed body as an 'industrial version of the Institute for Advanced Study'. The conference report that names RIAS is itself WADC Technical Report 57-216, produced under a funding agreement with the Wright Air Development Center.

Grade it honestly. This is a corporate research institute that studied gravitation, and nothing read shows it producing gravity-control results -- the Chapel Hill participants recorded their own concern about 'the absolute necessity to avoid identification with so-called anti-gravity research', and Louis Witten's documented output is classical general relativity. Its value here is the chain and the date, not a result.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 767 of 1082, score 21). 5 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C1/A1/D0.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** The participants list of the January 1957 Chapel Hill conference prints 'Witten, L. | RIAS, Inc., Baltimore' and 'Bass, R.W. | RIAS, Inc., Baltimore', establishing RIAS as a corporate entity in Baltimore with staff at the conference. Source: The Role of Gravitation in Physics: Report from the 1957 Chapel Hill Conference, participants list
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Louis Witten 'was the first researcher hired by RIAS, of the Glenn Martin Company.' The institute was 'industry-sponsored, and not ensconced in a university', in deliberate contrast with the Institute of Field Physics placed inside the University of North Carolina. Source: Dean Rickles, 'The Chapel Hill Conference in Context'
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** Nothing read shows RIAS producing gravity-control results. The Chapel Hill participants recorded a concern about 'the absolute necessity to avoid identification with so-called anti-gravity research', and Witten's documented output is classical general relativity. Source: Dean Rickles, 'The Chapel Hill Conference in Context'; AIP catalog abstract for the Louis Witten oral history
- **Tier 2 named person on the record.** RIAS was proposed by George S. Trimble of the Glenn L. Martin Company as an 'industrial version of the Institute for Advanced Study', explicitly to study gravity, in a letter to Bryce DeWitt dated 10 June 1955. Source: Trimble to Bryce DeWitt, 10 June 1955, quoted by Dean Rickles in the Edition Open Sources edition of the 1957 conference report

## The annual DoD special access program report to Congress

The annual DoD special access program report to Congress is a document on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 1988-03-01; axes C1 A3 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: report.

### What it is

The annual SAP report is the single document that would settle the central question of this board, and everything about how it is produced is documented. It is required by 10 U.S.C. 119(a)(1) by 1 March each year to the six defense committees, and must set out the total requested for DoD SAPs and, for each program in the budget, a brief description, major milestones, actual prior cost, and estimated cost across six fiscal years. DoDI 5205.11 assigns its production: the cognizant authorities 'shall prepare and submit annual reports and other required SAP information through the Director, DoD SAPCO, and the governance structure in order to report the state of DoD SAPs to congressional committees'; the Senior Review Group 'Ensures the accuracy of the SAP list submitted to Congress prior to submission of the SAP annual report'; and the SAPOC conducts 'an annual review and validation of SAP reports sent to the Congressional defense and intelligence committees'. The report is coordinated with the Assistant Secretary of Defense for Legislative Affairs under DoDI 5545.02. Programs identified in it are identified by unclassified nickname. Waived programs are, by definition, the ones removed from it under 10 U.S.C. 119(e), with their information going instead to eight to twelve named members; and under DoDD 5205.07 (2010) para 4.h the waived material 'shall remain in DoD custody' with no copies stored outside DoD repositories. The report has never been released, in whole or in redacted part, under FOIA. It is the highest value single target on the board: one document, produced annually since 1988, whose table of contents is the answer.

### The board's reading

The single most valuable unreleased document on this board. It exists. It has existed in annual editions since the first one fell due under 10 U.S.C. 119(a)(1). It lists, by unclassified nickname, every DoD special access program in the budget, with a brief description, milestones, historical cost and six years of cost estimates. Its accuracy is certified before submission by the Senior Review Group and validated annually by the Special Access Program Oversight Committee, chaired by the Deputy Secretary of Defense. It is coordinated with the Assistant Secretary of Defense for Legislative Affairs under DoDI 5545.02. Programs whose reporting has been waived under 119(e) are, by definition, absent from it, and the substitute material given to the chairs and ranking members 'shall remain in DoD custody' with no copies stored outside DoD repositories. Three things follow for anyone trying to force the record open. First, the assertion that the government does not know how many special access programs it has is false and is contradicted by DoD's own governance directive. Second, the difference between the count in the annual report and the count on the Senior Review Group's waived list is a single number that would, if produced, tell the public how much of the SAP enterprise is invisible to the committees as a whole. Third, that number has never been requested in public by any member of Congress at any hearing this board has recorded.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 106 of 1082, score 59). 5 connections, 2 of them custody/funding/programmatic, 1 official denial recorded against it. Axes C1/A3/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** CUSTODY. DoDD 5205.07 (2010) para 4.h: 'All waived SAP material submitted to Congress, to include annual reports and other products, shall remain in DoD custody. No copies shall be stored outside DoD repositories or control without the approval of the Secretary or Deputy Secretary of Defense.' The waived annex of the annual report never leaves the department. Source: https://web.archive.org/web/20220322050205/https://www.esd.whs.mil/Portals/54/Documents/DD/issuances/dodd/520507p.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** DoDD 5205.07 (2010) Enclosure 2 para 6: 'The CAs shall prepare and submit annual reports and other required SAP information through the Director, DoD SAPCO, and the governance structure in order to report the state of DoD SAPs to congressional committees, in accordance with Reference (c).' Source: DoDD 5205.07 (2010), Enclosure 2, para 6
- **Tier 1 documented.** DoDI 5205.11 para 5.d: 'Unclassified nicknames will be used in the annual SAP report to Congress.' Source: DoDI 5205.11, para 5.d

## Special Access Program Oversight Committee (SAPOC)

Special Access Program Oversight Committee (SAPOC) is a institution on the board; board tier A, named in sworn testimony or primary documents as directly inside the programme; dates 1994-01-05; axes C2 A3 D1; 5 connections.

In one line: oversight-body.

### What it is

The SAPOC was established on 5 January 1994, not in 2010. A Deputy Secretary of Defense information bulletin of November 1994 records that 'On January 5th, 1994, the Deputy Secretary of Defense (DEPSECDEF) significantly enhanced changes in the DoD Special Access Program (SAP) management and control structures' and that the plan 'standardized and formalized the SAP approval, termination, revalidation and restructuring process through the Special Access Program Oversight Committee (SAPOC). A Senior Review Group (SRG) was established to support the SAPOC principals'; the Joint Security Commission called it a 'recent formation' on 28 February 1994. DoD Directive 5205.07 of 1 July 2010 is therefore a later instrument that re-established a committee which had been running for sixteen years, and it survives in the 12 September 2024 reissue as 'the senior governing body of the DoD SAP Enterprise'. Under the 2013 instruction its chair is the Deputy Secretary of Defense, its vice-chair the Under Secretary of Defense for Acquisition, Technology and Logistics, and its executive secretary the Director of DoD SAPCO; its general membership is the Under Secretaries for Policy, Comptroller/CFO, Intelligence, and Personnel and Readiness, the Vice Chairman of the Joint Chiefs of Staff, the DoD Chief Information Officer, the General Counsel of the Department, the Director of Cost Assessment and Program Evaluation, the Under Secretaries of the Army, Navy and Air Force, the Vice Chiefs of Staff of the Army and Air Force, the Vice Chief of Naval Operations and the Assistant Commandant of the Marine Corps. Its three primary functions are to review issues on which the Senior Review Group could not reach unanimity, to ensure combatant command warfighting needs are reflected in SAP programmatics and budgets, and - the function that matters here - to 'Conduct an annual review and validation of SAP reports sent to the Congressional defense and intelligence committees'. In other words, the document Congress receives about special access programs is written and validated by a committee of roughly eighteen senior officials of the Department the report is about, chaired by the same official who signs the carve-outs. This is the body whose membership a witness alleging concealment would have to say was either complicit or itself deceived; DoD's own directive makes that a binary with no third option.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded A on a global pass (rank 108 of 1082, score 58). 5 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C2/A3/D1.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** A central Army special-access oversight office predates the 1994 reform by ten years. GAO/NSIAD-88-152 records: 'A DOD review of Army special access programs in 1983 revealed systemic weaknesses in various management areas, which resulted in establishment of a central oversight office in September 1984.' The same report states that in 1988 'The Deputy Under Secretary of Defense for Policy is required to maintain a listing of all special access programs, but does not do so because of security concerns', and that 'the Secretary's Office has no centralized office with cognizance of all special access programs'. The January 1994 dates this board carries stand; the Army aftermath began in 1983-84. Source: GAO/NSIAD-88-152, B-230522, 24 May 1988, Results in Brief and Recommendations, https://www.gao.gov/assets/nsiad-88-152.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** A commission chartered by the Secretary of Defense and the Director of Central Intelligence corroborated the formation within two months: 'Until recently there has been no procedure for centralized assessment of special program proposals submitted directly to the Deputy Secretary of Defense by the military departments. The recent formation of the DoD Special Access Program Oversight Committee, which the Commission fully supports, will ensure that every program is reviewed by a panel of senior officials prior to its establishment, and annually thereafter, to determine whether compartmentation for each program is still required.' Source: Joint Security Commission, Redefining Security: A Report to the Secretary of Defense and the Director of Central Intelligence, 28 February 1994, chapter 2, https://sgp.fas.org/library/jsc/chap2.html
- **Tier 1 documented.** DoDD 5205.07 (12 September 2024) retains the SAPOC as 'the senior governing body of the DoD SAP Enterprise for governance, management, and oversight of DoD SAPs', chaired by the Deputy Secretary of Defense. Source: DoDD 5205.07 (2024), section 3.2.a
- **Tier 1 documented.** DoDD 5205.07 (2010) para 4.b: 'The Special Access Program Oversight Committee (SAPOC), hereby established, shall advise and assist the Secretary of Defense (SecDef) and the Deputy Secretary of Defense with governance, management, and oversight of DoD SAPs, as provided in Enclosure 2.' Source: https://web.archive.org/web/20220322050205/https://www.esd.whs.mil/Portals/54/Documents/DD/issuances/dodd/520507p.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** DoDD 5205.07 (2010) para 4.b: 'The Special Access Program Oversight Committee (SAPOC), hereby established, shall advise and assist the Secretary of Defense (SecDef) and the Deputy Secretary of Defense with governance, management, and oversight of DoD SAPs.' Source: DoDD 5205.07 (2010), para 4.b
- **Tier 1 documented.** DoDD 5205.07 (2024) retains the SAPOC as the 'senior governing body' ensuring SAPs 'meet warfighter needs while protecting CPI', chaired by the Deputy Secretary of Defense with the Director, DoD SAPCO as Executive Secretary. Source: https://www.esd.whs.mil/Portals/54/Documents/DD/issuances/dodd/520507p.pdf
- **Tier 1 documented.** DoDI 5205.11 Enclosure 3 para 2.b(3): the SAPOC convenes at the direction of the Deputy Secretary of Defense to 'Conduct an annual review and validation of SAP reports sent to the Congressional defense and intelligence committees.' Source: DoDI 5205.11, Enclosure 3, para 2.b(3)

## Scientific Coalition for UAP Studies (SCU)

Scientific Coalition for UAP Studies (SCU) is a institution on the board; board tier C, contextual, real and relevant, no direct programme linkage on the record yet; dates 2017; axes C0 A0 D3; 5 connections.

In one line: 501(c)(3) research think tank; FOIA-driven sensor-record analysis with DOI-stamped publication.

### What it is

The Scientific Coalition for UAP Studies describes itself as 'a think tank of scientists, former military and law enforcement officials and other professionals who seek evidence based answers to UAP questions' and is a registered 501(c)(3). Its distinguishing practice is procedural: obtain the primary government sensor record by FOIA, reconstruct the event quantitatively, and deposit the study on Zenodo with a DOI so that it is time-stamped and citable rather than a PDF on a website. The resulting corpus is substantial - the 2010 Stephenville radar and witness study by Robert Powell and Glen Schulze; the 2015 analysis by Powell, Beall, Cates, Paulson and Hoffman of thermal imagery captured by a Department of Homeland Security Customs and Border Protection aircraft over Aguadilla, Puerto Rico in April 2013; Peter Reali's 2022 forensic analysis of the 'Rubber Duck' video; the 2023 Powell, Little, Hancock, Hasan and Truong study of reported shape, size, kinematics, electromagnetic effects and sound; Stephen Bruehl's 2025 cluster analysis of 216 selected reports; and the multi-year Hancock, Porritt and Grosvenor series correlating 1945-1975 UAP activity with the United States atomic warfare complex, including 'UAP Operational Presence 1945-1975' in April 2026. SCU personnel appear across the rest of the technical layer: Powell co-authored the Entropy kinematics paper with Knuth, and Sarah Little co-authors with both SCU and the Galileo Project.

### Why it holds its tier

Re-graded C on a global pass (rank 709 of 1082, score 23). 5 connections, 0 of them custody/funding/programmatic. Axes C0/A0/D3.

### The claims on the record

- **Tier 1 documented.** SCU is a registered 501(c)(3) charitable organisation. Source: explorescu.org
- **Tier 1 documented.** SCU published '2013 Aguadilla Puerto Rico UAP: The detailed analysis of an Unidentified Anomalous Phenomenon captured by the Department of Homeland Security' by Powell, Beall, Cates, Paulson and Hoffman. Source: SCU, Zenodo DOI 10.5281/zenodo.7844175
- **Tier 1 documented.** SCU published 'The Reported Shape, Size, Kinematics, Electromagnetic Effects, and Presence of Sound of Unidentified Aerial Phenomena' by Powell, Little, Hancock, Hasan and Truong on 7 December 2023. Source: SCU, Zenodo DOI 10.5281/zenodo.10287332
- **Tier 1 documented.** SCU's Hancock, Porritt and Grosvenor series analysing UAP activity against the United States atomic warfare complex 1945-1975 runs from 2023 through 'UAP Operational Presence 1945-1975' in April 2026. Source: SCU Zenodo record, DOI 10.5281/zenodo.7758498 and DOI 10.5281/zenodo.16299623
